In\\nmanaged mode, a material design change still requires Product Owner approval\\nbefore its code or test skeletons are implemented. Record the approval and\\nre-run the audit after that change. Do not mark the documentation audit\\ncomplete merely because files exist or their headings match.\\n\\n## P9. Demonstrate each sprint increment at handover\\n\\n### Purpose\\n\\nEvery sprint ends with a developer-led, live increment demonstration to the\\nProduct Owner before the Product Owner is asked to accept the sprint\\ndocumentation or close the sprint. The developer operates the actual product\\nor opens the actual delivered artifact in front of the Product Owner. A slide\\ndeck, recording, screenshot, test log, or claim that a command ran earlier\\ndoes not substitute for this demonstration. This adds a review step to the\\nexisting RUP wrap-up; it does not change the canonical sprint states, PBI\\nacceptance criteria, or Product Owner decision rights. A demonstration is\\nrequired even for a non-code-bearing iteration:\\nshow the actual delivered document, experiment, or decision outcome rather\\nthan pretending that an executable feature exists.\\n\\nThis local practice follows the [Scrum Guide's Sprint Review purpose](https://scrumguides.org/docs/scrumguide/v2020/2020-Scrum-Guide-US.pdf):\\ninspect the sprint outcome together and determine future adaptations in a\\nworking session.\\n\\nDemo day should leave the Product Owner confident and satisfied that the\\ntime and money invested produced an understandable, useful result. The\\nProduct Owner should be able to answer: what was promised, what was\\ndelivered, how does it work, what can a user accomplish now, what remains\\nuncertain, and what should happen next? Earn that confidence through direct\\ninspection and honest discussion. Record the Product Owner's actual reaction;\\nthe developer cannot declare the Product Owner satisfied on their behalf.\\n\\n### Prepare the demonstration\\n\\nThe developer owns and presents the handover. Prepare one coherent user\\njourney tied to the sprint goal and assigned PBIs. Identify local\\nprerequisites, exact actions, expected visible results, and one relevant\\nfailure or limitation to show. Run a preflight and rehearsal on the intended\\nenvironment before inviting the Product Owner. State benefits only when the\\ndemonstration or evidence supports them; do not invent a return on investment.\\n\\n### Run demo day\\n\\n1. Set the context in product language: the user problem, sprint goal, and\\n   specific outcome promised for this sprint.\\n2. Start with a known product or artifact state and real input. Perform the\\n   user actions in order. Show intermediate results and the final output so\\n   the Product Owner can follow the complete path from input to useful\\n   outcome. For model-backed behavior, use actual model inference when\\n   claiming model capability; label synthetic or injected data as a contract\\n   check.\\n3. Let the Product Owner inspect the result, try a step where practical,\\n   ask questions, and request a repeat or alternative input. Explain what\\n   can now be done with the increment and what still requires work.\\n4. After the journey, show the relevant failure or limitation and the\\n   supporting test or benchmark evidence. Explain which parts were delivered\\n   this sprint, what the investment established, and which questions remain\\n   for later decisions.\\n5. Discuss feedback and what to change next. In managed mode, ask for the\\n   Product Owner's explicit handover decision through the existing process.\\n\\nIf the live journey cannot run, record the blocker and arrange a corrected\\ndemonstration. Do not treat slides or earlier test evidence as a completed\\nlive demo.\\n\\n### Prepare the handover material\\n\\n#### One presentation pair and its supporting records\\n\\nMaintain one current presentation pair in `progress/sprint_N/`:\\n`sprint_N_increment_demo.pptx` and `sprint_N_increment_demo.md`. Their basenames\\nmust match. The Markdown file is the Product Owner's complete end-to-end\\nwalkthrough, including runnable steps and expected results, as well as the\\npresenter's slide references. It must lead a reader through the increment\\nwithout requiring them to assemble instructions from implementation records,\\nraw logs, screenshots or slide notes. Link this entry point prominently from\\nthe project README, sprint manual and handover record.\\n\\nKeep the operating reference in `progress/sprint_N/user_manual.md`. State\\nsupported tasks, prerequisites, recovery and known limits, and link to the\\npresentation walkthrough. A release-ready copy may be promoted to `docs/`\\nafter review, with its scope and links reconciled. Do not create a second\\nmanual in the repository root. Record the demonstration and Product Owner\\ndecision in `progress/sprint_N/sprint_N_handover.md`; place rehearsal logs,\\ncaptures, saved outputs and validation receipts in `progress/sprint_N/tests/`.\\n\\nUse the canonical PPTX path for revisions. Remove superseded working decks\\nfrom the user-facing delivery set when authorized; preserve necessary history\\nin Git and retained evidence. Do not remove a user's open-file lock or session\\ndata. Keep private slide-build intermediates outside the delivery set.\\n\\n#### The Markdown end-to-end walkthrough\\n\\nStart with the sprint goal, what the user will accomplish, and the real\\nstarting state. List the supported variations the sprint promised to exercise,\\nsuch as languages, backends, input types, optional stages or configuration\\nprofiles. Include their actual paths through the demonstration. For each\\nnumbered stage, provide the action or copyable command, its required inputs,\\nthe expected visible result, how the reader checks it, its relevant requirement\\nand PBI, and the matching slide or section. Show intermediate outputs as well\\nas the final result. Include inspection commands or product screens that make\\nsaved output understandable to a person; a UUID or file path alone is\\ninsufficient.\\n\\nFor a CLI product, provide a complete manual sequence in execution order.\\nEstablish the working directory, settings, dependencies, data and result store\\nbefore first use. Use staged, verified inputs; do not publish nonexistent\\nexample paths as working commands. When an input needs staging, include the\\nactual acquisition/preparation steps or a verified linked procedure. State\\nlanguage, duration and participant characteristics of demo recordings and\\nretain source URLs and reference provenance in the fixture/evidence record.\\nSpeech clips cannot establish a multi-participant meeting capability. Keep\\ntest-only reference injection in the test documentation; demonstrate product\\nbehavior with the actual inputs and models.\\n\\nKeep dependent commands in one named shell session, or print complete commands\\nfor another Terminal with actual IDs and paths. Explain prompts, required\\noperator input and the visible saved change. Do not rely on undefined shell\\nvariables, an unverified personal identity or invented corrections. For a GUI\\nstage, describe the controls to use, what to listen to or inspect, how to save,\\nundo or restore, and when an external CLI change requires a refresh or restart.\\nShow relevant playback, selection and correction controls where delivered.\\nLabel optional steps, the effects of skipping them, and retained neutral or\\nunknown values. Explain failure/recovery branches and any invalidation of\\nderived results after a correction or configuration change.\\n\\nWhen a guided demo runner is provided, expose one documented entry point with\\npreflight, live and unattended-rehearsal modes. Preflight checks actual data, settings, model/tool dependencies and\\nrequired retained evidence. Failed model\\nvalidation may be a demonstrated experiment result, and must remain visible.\\nUse the project's working language for instructions and status messages;\\nretain the source language of recordings and transcripts.\\n\\nFor a non-executable iteration, use the same walkthrough structure to open,\\ninspect and assess the delivered documents, decisions or experiment results.\\nState which operating steps do not apply and why.\\n\\n#### PPTX structure and readability\\n\\nUse a readable, consistent presentation layout with clear titles, adequate\\ntype size and contrast, sufficient spacing, and short text blocks. Add an\\nagenda near the beginning and titled divider slides between major sections.\\nKeep the following content in this order, grouping sections proportionately\\nto the sprint:\\n\\n1. Delivered and unfinished backlog items, conclusions, priorities and the\\n   Product Owner decision sought. Explain the intended outcome, observed failure, supporting evidence,\\nuser impact, attempted repair and its measured result. Preserve the earlier\\ndesign and failure record so the reader can follow the evolution. Include quality,\\ncorrectness, coverage, runtime, resource use and other measures relevant to\\nthe sprint's risks. Preserve raw historical captures and label later repeats accurately; a later screenshot does not record an earlier action. Keep requested screenshots rather than substituting text summaries without the Product Owner's agreement.\n\n### Repository trees and code listings\n\nUse Courier for directory trees, command listings and code blocks in this project's PPTX decks; keep the deck's existing typography for explanatory prose. Keep listings editable. Show directory hierarchy with aligned tree branches and one entry per line, rather than combining sibling paths with punctuation. An abbreviated tree must distinguish omitted content from literal paths and must reflect the repository actually inspected.\n\nSize the listing frame for its longest line. Do not let paths, filenames or command arguments wrap unintentionally, especially at hyphens. Prefer shortening the displayed scope, moving detail into another slide or separating explanation from the listing before shrinking text below a readable presentation size. Preserve complete filenames and meaningful hierarchy.\n\nInspect the exported listing slides at presentation size in the intended viewer when available. Check alignment, intact filenames, command line breaks, margins and readability; package/layout checks alone do not establish this. Repair and inspect again after a font change, because monospaced text can be wider than the original font. When inserting slides, update page numbers and the maintained walkthrough's slide map.\n# Product Owner preferences\n\nThis is the project-local, user-editable preferences file for RUP Strikes\nBack. The Product Owner can ask Codex to add or change a preference here.\nEach preference states its scope in ordinary language. These preferences\nguide presentation of new or revised project artifacts; they do not change\nProduct Backlog priorities, sprint status, or required process state.\n\n## Markdown presentation — 2026-10-01\n\nFor narrative Markdown documents, design reviews, and user-facing reports,\nwrite in paragraphs with clear headings. Do not use Markdown tables for\ncomparisons, mappings, or acceptance evidence in those documents. A numbered\nlist is welcome when I ask for enumerated arguments or when order matters.\n\nKeep `PROGRESS_BOARD.md` as the RUP method's required four-column Markdown\ntable. Do not reformat completed historical sprint evidence solely to apply\nthis preference.\n\n## Metric terminology — 2026-10-04\n\nUse the full name “Word Error Rate” in explanations, presentations and\nproject-authored narrative documents. Do not shorten this metric to initials.\nRetain machine-readable field names and historical raw test evidence.\n\n## Working style and sprint boundaries — 2026-10-16\n\nAvoid unnecessary bureaucracy. Keep sprint records focused on useful decisions,\ndeliverables and evidence; do not turn conversation wording into a running\nincident account. Communicate directly and keep routine process detail brief.\n\nWork only on the current sprint. Do not edit other sprints' artifacts. Shared\nproduct documents and the current sprint's plan/board entries may be updated\nas required for its authorized work.\n\n\n## Portable operating instructions — 2026-10-17\n\nUse Git repository discovery to position commands at the repository root. Never\nhard-code a developer's home directory or host-specific checkout path in\nmaintained instructions or scripts. Preserve historical raw evidence as recorded;\nnew executable examples must be portable. Keep cleanup/preparation and walkthrough\nchapters/scripts synchronized, with explicit scope and preservation behavior.\n\n## File and directory viewing — 2026-10-20\n\nUse the VS Code `code` command in maintained operating instructions to view\nfiles and directories on macOS and Windows. Prefer `code path/to/file` and\n`code path/to/directory` over platform-specific viewer commands such as macOS\n`open` or `open -t`. State that the `code` command must be available on PATH.\nThis viewing preference does not imply that all other shell commands or\nruntime prerequisites are portable between operating systems.\n\n## Explain encapsulated scripts before invocation — 2026-11-04\n\nBefore each script invocation in operating instructions or a demonstration,\nprovide an introduction/explanation subsection describing the code it hides.\nFor SQL files, explain the significant SQL, PL/SQL and native tool commands,\ntheir inputs, connection context, effects, checks and failure behavior. For\nother scripts or workflow automation, explain non-obvious techniques and how\nthey relate to the visible manual operations. Address an experienced engineer;\nomit basic Bash, SQL and command-line tutorials. Repeated invocations may refer\nto an earlier explanation, but must identify their particular purpose before\nexecution. Encapsulation must not obscure the operation being demonstrated.\nInclude the complete SQL source in a fenced SQL block in the document before\nits invocation, alongside the explanation; prose, selected command names and\nlinks to a source file alone do not satisfy this requirement. Keep displayed\nsource synchronized with the executable file. Repeat the small SQL source\nwhen a separate exercise needs it to be understandable in place.\n\n## Presentation agenda and section headers — 2026-11-05\n\nAlways include an agenda near the start of a multi-slide presentation, immediately\nafter the cover when present, and a dedicated section header slide before each\nmajor section. Apply this when creating and editing PPTX decks. Keep agenda\nlabels, section titles, slide numbers and walkthrough references synchronized.\nReuse existing agenda/divider slides rather than adding duplicates. Follow an\nexplicit later request for a different structure or fixed slide count.\n\n## Presentation closing slide — 2026-11-05\n\nAlways end a multi-slide presentation with a dedicated closing slide such as\n“Thank you” or “Q&A”. Apply this to new decks and edits, preserving an existing\nclosing slide when present. Match the deck style and keep the slide map current.\nA later explicit request for a different ending takes precedence.\n# RUP Patch — Method Adoption and Iteration Semantics\n\nThis is an optional local wrapper over the canonical RUP Strikes Back method in the `RUPStrikesBack` submodule. A project may use RUP Strikes Back without this directory. When this wrapper is present, its policies refine or override the generic method only where stated below.\n\n## P0. Include RUP Strikes Back as a controlled project method\n\nThe project adopts RUP Strikes Back through the `RUPStrikesBack` Git submodule, tracked on the selected method branch and pinned by this repository’s Git submodule revision. The submodule is the source for the generic manager, phase-agent procedures, sprint/backlog formats, and generic rules.\n\nThe project root contains the local adoption layer:\n\n1. `.gitmodules` registers the RUP Strikes Back submodule and selected branch.\n2. `AGENTS.md` tells Codex where the method is and when to use it.\n3. `RUPStrikesBack.patch/RUP_patch.md` records project-specific method refinements and overrides; the root `RUP_patch.md` is its compatibility entry point.\n4. `.agents/skills/rup-strikes-back/` is the Codex cycle entry point, while `rup-backlog`, `rup-sprint`, and `rup-progress` are mandatory artifact adapters. Their references map to the canonical manager, command, and phase procedures in the submodule.\n5. `BACKLOG.md` holds Product Owner priorities and backlog-item acceptance signals.\n6. `PLAN.md` holds RUP Strikes Back sprint definitions, statuses, modes, and quality expectations.\n\nDo not copy the submodule’s `AGENTS.md`, `HUMANS.md`, `RUP_patch.md`, or Claude command files into the project root. The local `AGENTS.md`, this patch, and the Codex skill provide the project-specific integration layer. The submodule remains the canonical generic agentic method; Codex adapters provide host-specific invocation and apply local refinements.\n\nUpdate the submodule only through an explicit Product Owner request. Before adopting an update, review the changed method rules, confirm that this patch and the Codex wrapper still apply, and commit the resulting submodule revision together with any required local changes. Do not update the method during an active sprint unless the Product Owner explicitly treats it as a process change.\n\n## P0.phase. Use stable phase-based sprint IDs\n\nBy Product Owner direction on 7 October 2026, project sprint scheduling uses\n`I.n` for Inception and `E.n` for Elaboration. The Product Owner\nalso designated `ES.1` for the deferred Elaboration baseline. The ordinal advances within its phase prefix; inserting work in\nanother phase does not renumber a sprint. The current mapping is recorded in\n`PLAN.md`. In this project, these phase IDs override the generic numeric,\ngap-free sprint-number rule and are the labels in `PLAN.md` and\n`PROGRESS_BOARD.md`.\n\nBy Product Owner direction on 9 October 2026, historical evidence directories\nalso use phase IDs, with maintained links and scripts updated to those paths.\nSprint evidence uses the phase ID in its path, for example `progress/E.5/`.\n`PLAN.md` records the former numeric labels. Historical filenames, raw outputs,\nlogs and decisions retain their original content; paths recorded inside raw\nevidence describe the location at execution time. `ES` is still Elaboration.\nReserve `C.n` for work actually classified as Construction.\n\n## P0.0. Establish the Git baseline during bootstrap\n\nBootstrap validates that the project is a Git repository and has an `origin` remote. If Git is absent, bootstrap initializes the repository and uses `main` as its default branch. If `origin` is absent, bootstrap creates a private repository on GitHub.com using the authenticated GitHub account, names it after the project directory, and configures it as `origin`. An existing origin is preserved; bootstrap does not rewrite it or force-push.\n\nAfter installing only the method and generic-bootstrap artifacts, bootstrap creates one semantic baseline commit and pushes the current branch to `origin`. This authorization applies to the bootstrap flow itself. Subsequent remote repositories, remote changes, and pushes still require an explicit Product Owner request.\n\n## P0.1. Initialize process artifacts when needed\n\n`BACKLOG.md` and `PLAN.md` are created before the first sprint. Progress artifacts, including `PROGRESS_BOARD.md` and sprint-specific records under `progress/`, are created and maintained by the RUP Strikes Back cycle when a sprint starts; do not create placeholder progress files in advance unless the Product Owner explicitly requests a pre-sprint artifact. Such a file does not start the sprint.\n\nBefore the first code-bearing sprint, define the project’s test profile in this patch or a referenced project rule. The project uses `docs/test-profile.md` for this purpose. It must map the generic test and regression fields to the actual project’s build, smoke-test, unit-test, integration-test, and applicable operational-validation commands. Do not claim that the submodule’s sample shell-test commands apply to the project. A test-profile template does not satisfy this gate: PBI-009 must replace its pending command fields with the candidate architecture's actual commands before Sprint 2 starts.\n\n## P0.2. Generic artifacts bootstrap Sprint 0\n\n`RUPStrikesBack.patch/bootstrap/` contains reusable RUP roadmap, backlog, sprint-plan, and test-profile templates, a generic `AGENTS.md`, and Codex skill adapters. `RUPStrikesBack.patch/BOOTSTRAP_PROMPT.md` is the operator-only bootstrap prompt and is deliberately kept outside the overlay. These artifacts are inputs to Sprint 0 only; they are not product requirements or active product backlog items. The bootstrap plan preselects Sprint 0 with `Status: Progress`, but execution still requires an explicit Product Owner request.\n\nAt project initialization, overlay the complete contents of `RUPStrikesBack.patch/bootstrap/` onto the project root, including hidden directories. Do not copy `RUPStrikesBack.patch/BOOTSTRAP_PROMPT.md` into the project root. The Product Owner explicitly requests execution of the preselected Sprint 0 and supplies the initial product intent through the working conversation. PBI-001 creates and accepts the project `README.md` as the vision product. Sprint 0 then derives this project’s real roadmap and proposes the next sprint from that vision. The generic later-sprint entries are initial hypotheses: Sprint 0 or subsequent iterations may retain, refine, split, reorder, or replace them. After Sprint 0, the root project artifacts—not the generic templates—are the sources of truth for the project’s scope and delivery work.\n\nThe generic bootstrap is deliberately tailored over its first three iterations. Sprint 0 proposes an initial lifecycle and iteration plan from the vision. Sprint 1 tests and refines that plan against business context, requirements, scope, risks, and an initial architecture. Sprint 2 refines it again from technical-validation evidence and the Lifecycle Architecture assessment. In each of these iterations, the agent must propose additions, removals, mergers, splits, reordered work, or a simpler path when the project’s size, uncertainty, existing assets, constraints, or risk profile justify it. In managed mode, the Product Owner accepts each material plan change. The plan must record the decision and rationale; generic tasks are defaults, never mandatory ceremony.\n\n## P0.3. Place project artifacts by lifecycle role\n\nThe root `README.md` holds the accepted product vision and project orientation. `docs/` holds durable shared project deliverables created and accepted during sprints, such as requirements, architecture, decisions, and operating documentation.\n\n`progress/<phase-ID>/` directories hold evidence and review artifacts for one sprint, including migrated historical evidence. `tmp/` holds provisional material retained only for reference; it is not a source of truth and must be recreated in `docs/` or another approved location when a sprint formally delivers it. `RUPStrikesBack.patch/bootstrap/` remains the reusable bootstrap input described above.\n\n## P0.4. Anchor progress-board items to an assigned sprint\n\n`PROGRESS_BOARD.md` follows the canonical four-column form and tracks both a\nsprint's real-time status and the real-time statuses of backlog items assigned\nto that sprint. Every backlog-item row must name the Sprint that contains the\nitem in `PLAN.md`; an unscheduled item never receives a standalone progress\nboard row.\n\nA Product Owner may promote an accepted review or feedback proposal into the\nroot backlog with `Status: Proposed`. It remains there, without a progress\nboard row, until the Product Owner prioritizes and assigns it to a named Sprint\nin `PLAN.md`. The sprint procedure then creates its board row. This refines the\ncanonical `/backlog add` instruction only for unscheduled proposals.\n\n## P1. Apply the RUP Strikes Back cycle proportionately\n\nThe Codex RUP Strikes Back manager's complete contracting, analysis/design, construction, quality-validation, and wrap-up pipeline is primarily a code-bearing Construction-iteration workflow. It should run fully when a sprint creates or materially changes production code.\n\nSprint 0 and Inception iterations use the same collaboration discipline, but produce vision, requirements, plans, risk evidence, and reviews rather than forcing implementation. An Elaboration iteration may construct a prototype and run technical tests when that is needed to retire risk, but it need not produce a production feature. In non-code-bearing iterations, construction and automated-test gates may be not applicable; the plan and sprint evidence must state the reason and define the appropriate review or experiment instead.\n\nThis is not a waterfall handoff. Every iteration consciously considers the relevant RUP disciplines, at a depth proportional to its purpose, risk, and lifecycle phase. The RUP Strikes Back workflow inside an iteration does not itself advance the product from one project-level RUP phase to the next.\n\n## P1.1. Use progressive plan refinement as a first-class outcome\n\nThe plan is an evolving project artifact, not a one-time bootstrap output. Sprint 0 creates the initial project plan. Sprint 1 establishes or revises the Inception plan and evaluates the Lifecycle Objectives decision. Sprint 2 establishes or revises the Construction plan from technical evidence and evaluates the Lifecycle Architecture decision. Later iterations continue to refine scope, risks, ordering, quality expectations, and release plans when evidence requires it.\n\n## P1.2. Materialize durable Inception requirements and architecture artifacts\n\nWhen an Inception iteration establishes the initial requirements, PBI-005\nmaterializes `docs/srs.md` as the Software Requirements Specification (SRS).\nIt records the accepted system boundary, stakeholders, use cases and success\ncriteria, functional and non-functional requirements, initial release scope,\nconstraints, assumptions, and risks needed to guide the next iteration.\n\nPBI-009 materializes `docs/architecture.md` as the accepted candidate\narchitecture. It explains how the proposed architecture supports the SRS,\nidentifies the assumptions requiring Elaboration validation, and defines the\nproject's concrete test-profile commands before a code-bearing iteration\nstarts. An accepted candidate architecture is an accepted direction for\nvalidation, not evidence that its technical risks have been retired.\n\nThese artifacts are created from the accepted project vision during Inception;\nthey are not blank bootstrap templates. Later iterations refine them when\nvalidation evidence or product decisions change their content. The Inception\nreview evaluates them together with the Product Backlog and test profile.\n\n### Keep materialized documents connected\n\nBy Product Owner direction on 2026-10-18, every new or materially revised project document must identify its related authoritative documents with descriptive, actionable links. Add a reciprocal reference in the related maintained document so humans and agents can navigate from either side. State the relationship and which document owns requirements, design, operating steps or evidence; avoid duplicating specifications that can drift. Use section links where useful.\n\nWhen a source decision, interface, path or status changes, review the connected documents and update affected descriptions, links and backlinks in the same increment. Preserve historical sprint evidence rather than rewriting it; link to its successor/current interpretation from maintained guidance. Validate link targets and relevant anchors during P8, and explicitly label drafts, planned behavior and remaining gaps. For backlog definitions, retain the canonical concise item format: link from the elaboration/delivery guide to the item and place reciprocal navigation in the maintained project orientation, rather than embedding implementation paths in the backlog. This is the local materialization rule; it does not authorize changing the canonical method submodule or unrelated sprints.\n\n## P1.3. Adopt RUP analysis and use-case modeling explicitly\n\nBy explicit Product Owner instruction on 2026-10-16, RUP Strikes Back is the project's agentic adaptation of the Rational Unified Process (RUP). Apply RUP's use-case-driven, architecture-centric, iterative and risk-driven approach throughout delivery. The implementor is responsible for performing the analysis and explaining technical recommendations; the Product Owner supplies product intent, priorities and acceptance decisions.\n\nIdentify the system boundary and external interactions before designing the solution. The SRS must contain a separate **Actors** chapter that explicitly enumerates human roles and relevant external-system actors, gives each a stable identifier, and describes its responsibilities and goals. Distinguish actors from stakeholders, internal system components and individual people occupying the same role. Do not substitute scattered references to participants for an actor model.\n\nDefine actor-goal use cases covering the representative end-to-end journeys. Each use case must identify its primary actor, supporting actors where applicable, observable outcome and success criterion. Describe triggers/preconditions, the main flow, material alternative/failure flows and postconditions for architecturally significant or high-risk cases. Identify the remaining cases at a proportionate level during Inception and refine them from evidence during Elaboration. Include onboarding, environment preparation, ordinary work, integration, operation and recovery when they are part of the accepted system boundary; technical pipeline steps alone do not describe a complete actor journey.\n\nKeep requirements focused on externally observable behavior. Put implementation mechanisms and technical recommendations in the architecture/design and trace them to actor goals, requirements, risks and validation evidence. Review actor coverage and complete journeys before presenting the Inception baseline for acceptance; missing actors or unexplained gaps must be corrected. Scale detail to the project's size and phase under P1; use diagrams when they improve understanding and avoid duplicating the same specification across artifacts.\n\nGeneral RUP supplies the analysis discipline; the canonical RUP Strikes Back procedures and this local patch govern artifact routing, approvals and sprint transitions. References: [IBM Rational RUP best practices](https://public.dhe.ibm.com/software/rational/web/whitepapers/2003/rup_bestpractices.pdf), [actors in modeling](https://www.ibm.com/docs/en/dma?topic=diagrams-actors), and [use-case specification outline](https://www.ibm.com/docs/en/engineering-lifecycle-management-suite/doors-next/7.2.0?topic=cases-use-case-specification-outline).\n\n## P1.4. Use core technologies in their native areas\n\nBy explicit Product Owner direction on 2026-11-26, every sprint, including\nE.16, must use selected core technologies for their native responsibilities.\nDo not replace their existing capabilities with custom orchestration,\nframeworks or competing control planes. External logic and scripts may supply\nsmall checks, adapters and glue; they support the core technology and never\nreplace it. Do not overengineer or reinvent the wheel.\n\nDesign and review must identify which native technology owns each operation,\njustify any helper by the narrow gap it fills, and remove duplicate lifecycle\nlogic. The P8 audit verifies that implementation follows those boundaries.\nIf the selected core technology cannot satisfy an accepted requirement, stop\nimplementation and report the mismatch, exact evidence and alternatives to\nthe Product Owner. A Product Owner decision is required before substituting\ntechnology or building a custom replacement, including in YOLO mode. Do not\n\nStatus: Done\nMode: YOLO\nTest: smoke, unit, integration\nRegression: smoke, unit, integration\n\nMeasure cold and warm feedback, queue delay and resource demand for the complete JDK, SQLcl, utPLSQL and Oracle workload on the reproducible E.17 baseline. Compare serial and concurrent execution to recommend runner capacity and caching with declared sample counts, measurement limits and unchanged mandatory validation.\n\nBacklog Items:\n\n* PBI-086. Measure complete pipeline feedback and runner capacity\n\n## Sprint E.19 - Validate catalogued JavaScript support actions\n\nStatus: Planned\nMode: YOLO\nTest: smoke, unit, integration\nRegression: smoke, unit, integration\n\nBy Product Owner correction on 2026-11-26, retain this validation in\nElaboration as E.19, at the end of the current queue after E.18, rather than\nopening a Construction sprint. Scheduling does not activate the sprint or accept the Lifecycle\nArchitecture milestone.\n\nBy Product Owner direction on 2026-11-26, compare the JavaScript actions with\nthe existing Python solution on equivalent workloads and runner conditions.\nRecord tool versions, sample counts and cold/warm timings, separating runtime\nsetup, check execution and overall workflow time. rstyczynski  staff          96 Jul 11 08:00 visualizations\ndrwxr-xr-x@   3 rstyczynski  staff          96 Aug  6 12:17 worktrees\n01a2190a-d633-7fe2-878a-23726aa58c72\n# /sprint — Sprint Management\n\nManage sprints in `PLAN.md`.\n\n## Usage\n\n```\n/sprint create [<N>] [--title <title>] [--items <ID1,ID2,...>]\n/sprint status [<N>]\n/sprint close [<N>]\n/sprint start [<N>]\n```\n\n## Operations\n\n### create\n\nCreate a new sprint entry in `PLAN.md`.\n\n**Steps:**\n1. Read `PLAN.md` to find next sprint number (or use provided N)\n2. Read `BACKLOG.md` to validate item IDs\n3. Ask user for:\n   - Title (short description of sprint goal)\n   - Mode: `managed` or `YOLO`\n   - Test levels: `smoke`, `unit`, `integration`, or `none`\n   - Regression levels: `smoke`, `unit`, `integration`, or `none`\n   - Backlog items to include (if not provided via --items)\n4. Validate all required fields present\n5. Append sprint to `PLAN.md`\n6. Update `PROGRESS_BOARD.md` — add items with status \"Planned\"\n\n### status\n\nShow sprint status.\n\n**Steps:**\n1. Read `PLAN.md`\n2. If N provided, show that sprint; otherwise show active sprint (Status: Progress)\n3. Display: status, mode, test/regression requirements, items, progress from PROGRESS_BOARD.md\n\n### start\n\nStart a sprint (transition from Planned to Progress).\n\n**Steps:**\n1. Verify sprint exists and has Status: Planned\n2. Update sprint status to \"Progress\" in `PLAN.md`\n3. Update `PROGRESS_BOARD.md` — items to \"under_analysis\"\n\n### close\n\nComplete a sprint.\n\n**Steps:**\n1. Verify all quality gates passed (check `progress/sprint_N/sprint_N_tests.md`)\n2. Update sprint status to \"Done\" in `PLAN.md`\n3. Update `PROGRESS_BOARD.md` — move items to \"Done\"\n4. Optionally prompt for retrospective notes\n\n---\n\n## Format\n\nSee `rules/generic/sprint_definition.md` for full specification.\n\n```markdown\n## Sprint <N> - <Title>\n\nStatus: Planned | Progress | Done\nMode: managed | YOLO\nTest: <smoke | unit | integration | none>\nRegression: <smoke | unit | integration | none>\n\n<Optional: 1-2 sentences of context>\n\nBacklog Items:\n\n* <ID>. <Title>\n```\n\n## Required Fields\n\n| Field | Values | Default |\n|-------|--------|---------|\n| Status | `Planned`, `Progress`, `Done` | `Planned` |\n| Mode | `managed`, `YOLO` | `managed` |\n| Test | `smoke`, `unit`, `integration`, `none` | `unit, integration` |\n| Regression | `smoke`, `unit`, `integration`, `none` | `unit, integration` |\n\n## Validation\n\nBefore saving, verify:\n\n| Check | Rule |\n|-------|------|\n| Sprint N | Sequential, no gaps |\n| Status | Valid value |\n| Mode | Valid value |\n| Test | Required, valid values |\n| Regression | Required, valid values |\n| Items exist | All IDs must exist in BACKLOG.md |\n| No duplicates | Item cannot be in multiple active sprints |\n\n## Status Transitions\n\n```\nPlanned → Progress → Done\n                  → Failed\n```\n\n- `Planned → Progress`: When `/sprint start` or `/rup-manager` begins\n- `Progress → Done`: When `/sprint close` after all gates pass\n- `Progress → Failed`: When quality gates fail after retries exhausted\n# Sprint Definition\n\nA sprint entry in `PLAN.md` describes a unit of work the team commits to delivering. It must state what will be tested and what must not regress — so the implementor knows the quality bar before writing a line of code.\n\n---\n\n## Format\n\n```markdown\n## Sprint <N> - <Title>\n\nStatus: Planned | Progress | Done\nMode: managed | YOLO\nTest: <smoke | unit | integration | none>\nRegression: <smoke | unit | integration | none>\n\n<Optional: 1-2 sentences of context if the sprint is non-obvious.>\n\nBacklog Items:\n\n* <ID>. <Title>\n```\n\n---\n\n## Fields\n\n| Field | Required | Values | Description |\n|-------|----------|--------|-------------|\n| Status | Yes | `Planned`, `Progress`, `Done` | Current state of the sprint |\n| Mode | Yes | `managed`, `YOLO` | `managed` = full RUP gates; `YOLO` = autonomous, minimal ceremony |\n| Test | Yes | Comma-separated list | Which test levels to run for new code |\n| Regression | Yes | Comma-separated list | Which existing tests to re-run after new-code gates pass |\n\n### Test Parameter Values\n\n| Value | Meaning |\n|-------|---------|\n| `smoke` | Quick critical checks in `tests/smoke/`. Fast gate to determine \"is it testable?\" |\n| `unit` | Unit tests in `tests/unit/`. Tests individual functions. |\n| `integration` | Integration tests in `tests/integration/`. End-to-end scenarios. |\n| `none` | No new tests. For documentation-only or review-only sprints. |\n\n**Defaults if omitted:** `Test: unit, integration`\n\n### Regression Parameter Values\n\n| Value | Meaning |\n|-------|---------|\n| `smoke` | Re-run smoke tests only. Fastest regression. |\n| `unit` | Re-run all unit tests (old + new). Good balance. |\n| `integration` | Re-run all integration tests. Slowest but most thorough. |\n| `none` | Skip regression. Only for experimental/throwaway sprints. |\n\n**Defaults if omitted:** `Regression: unit, integration`\n\n### Optional: Regression Scope\n\nLimit regression to specific component:\n\n```markdown\nRegression scope: emit\n```\n\nAvailable scopes map to `tests/manifests/component_<scope>.manifest`.\n\n---\n\n## Constraints\n\n| Rule | Rationale |\n|------|-----------|\n| `Test` and `Regression` are required | They define exit criteria — no ambiguity |\n| No design in sprint entry | Design belongs in elaboration document |\n| No sub-tasks | Sub-tasks emerge during phases |\n| No implementation notes | Implementation belongs in construction |\n| One coherent deliverable | Bundle items only when tightly coupled |\n\n---\n\n## Status Transitions\n\n```\nPlanned → Progress → Done\n```\n\n| Transition | Trigger |\n|------------|---------|\n| `Planned → Progress` | When `/rup-cycle` or Phase 1 starts |\n| `Progress → Done` | When all quality gates pass and Phase 5 completes |\n| `Progress → Failed` | When retries exhausted and gates fail |\n\n---\n\n## Examples\n\n### Good Example\n\n```markdown\n## Sprint 8 - curl backend for emit.sh\n\nStatus: Planned\nMode: YOLO\nTest: unit\nRegression: unit\n\nBacklog Items:\n\n* SLI-11. Alternative HTTP backend for emit.sh\n```\n\n**Why it's good:**\n- Clear title\n- All required fields present\n- Test and Regression specify quality bar\n- Single focused backlog item\n\n### Good Example — Full Suite\n\n```markdown\n## Sprint 7 - Critical security fix\n\nStatus: Progress\nMode: managed\nTest: smoke, unit, integration\nRegression: smoke, unit, integration\n\nSecurity vulnerability requires thorough testing at all levels.\n\nBacklog Items:\n\n* SEC-42. Fix authentication bypass in API gateway\n```\n\n**Why it's good:**\n- Context explains why full testing\n- Mode is managed (human oversight for security)\n- Maximum test coverage appropriate for risk\n\n### Good Example — Documentation Only\n\n```markdown\n## Sprint 9 - API documentation update\n\nStatus: Planned\nMode: YOLO\nTest: none\nRegression: smoke\n\nBacklog Items:\n\n* DOC-15. Update API reference for v2 endpoints\n```\n\n**Why it's good:**\n- `Test: none` is appropriate — no code changes\n- `Regression: smoke` provides quick sanity check\n- YOLO mode for documentation work\n\n### Bad Example — Missing Test and Regression\n\n```markdown\n## Sprint 8 - curl backend for emit.sh\n\nStatus: Planned\nMode: YOLO\n\nBacklog Items:\n\n* SLI-11. Split emit.sh into emit_oci.sh and emit_curl.sh\n```\n\n**Why it's bad:**\n- Missing `Test:` field — quality bar undefined\n- Missing `Regression:` field — regression scope undefined\n- Implementor doesn't know when they're done\n\n---\n\n## Validation Checklist\n\nBefore adding to PLAN.md, verify:\n\n- [ ] Sprint number is sequential (no gaps)\n- [ ] Status is one of: Planned, Progress, Done\n- [ ] Mode is one of: managed, YOLO\n- [ ] Test field is present with valid values\n- [ ] Regression field is present with valid values\n- [ ] All backlog item IDs exist in BACKLOG.md\n- [ ] No item appears in multiple active sprints\n- [ ] Title is concise and descriptive\n",

Include RUP Strikes Back as a controlled project method\\n\\nThe project adopts RUP Strikes Back through the `RUPStrikesBack` Git submodule, tracked on the selected method branch and pinned by this repository’s Git submodule revision. The submodule is the source for the generic manager, phase-agent procedures, sprint/backlog formats, and generic rules.\\n\\nThe project root contains the local adoption layer:\\n\\n1. `.gitmodules` registers the RUP Strikes Back submodule and selected branch.\\n2. `AGENTS.md` tells Codex where the method is and when to use it.\\n3. `RUPStrikesBack.patch/RUP_patch.md` records project-specific method refinements and overrides; the root `RUP_patch.md` is its compatibility entry point.\\n4. `.agents/skills/rup-strikes-back/` is the Codex cycle entry point, while `rup-backlog`, `rup-sprint`, and `rup-progress` are mandatory artifact adapters. Their references map to the canonical manager, command, and phase procedures in the submodule.\\n5. `BACKLOG.md` holds Product Owner priorities and backlog-item acceptance signals.\\n6. `PLAN.md` holds RUP Strikes Back sprint definitions, statuses, modes, and quality expectations.\\n\\nDo not copy the submodule’s `AGENTS.md`, `HUMANS.md`, `RUP_patch.md`, or Claude command files into the project root. The local `AGENTS.md`, this patch, and the Codex skill provide the project-specific integration layer. The submodule remains the canonical generic agentic method; Codex adapters provide host-specific invocation and apply local refinements.\\n\\nUpdate the submodule only through an explicit Product Owner request. Before adopting an update, review the changed method rules, confirm that this patch and the Codex wrapper still apply, and commit the resulting submodule revision together with any required local changes. Do not update the method during an active sprint unless the Product Owner explicitly treats it as a process change.\\n\\n## P0.phase. Use stable phase-based sprint IDs\\n\\nBy Product Owner direction on 7 October 2026, project sprint scheduling uses\\n`I.n` for Inception and `E.n` for Elaboration. The Product Owner\\nalso designated `ES.1` for the deferred Elaboration baseline. The ordinal advances within its phase prefix; inserting work in\\nanother phase does not renumber a sprint. The current mapping is recorded in\\n`PLAN.md`. In this project, these phase IDs override the generic numeric,\\ngap-free sprint-number rule and are the labels in `PLAN.md` and\\n`PROGRESS_BOARD.md`.\\n\\nBy Product Owner direction on 9 October 2026, historical evidence directories\\nalso use phase IDs, with maintained links and scripts updated to those paths.\\nSprint evidence uses the phase ID in its path, for example `progress/E.5/`.\\n`PLAN.md` records the former numeric labels. Historical filenames, raw outputs,\\nlogs and decisions retain their original content; paths recorded inside raw\\nevidence describe the location at execution time. `ES` is still Elaboration.\\nReserve `C.n` for work actually classified as Construction.\\n\\n## P0.0. Establish the Git baseline during bootstrap\\n\\nBootstrap validates that the project is a Git repository and has an `origin` remote. If Git is absent, bootstrap initializes the repository and uses `main` as its default branch. If `origin` is absent, bootstrap creates a private repository on GitHub.com using the authenticated GitHub account, names it after the project directory, and configures it as `origin`. An existing origin is preserved; bootstrap does not rewrite it or force-push.\\n\\nAfter installing only the method and generic-bootstrap artifacts, bootstrap creates one semantic baseline commit and pushes the current branch to `origin`. This authorization applies to the bootstrap flow itself. Subsequent remote repositories, remote changes, and pushes still require an explicit Product Owner request.\\n\\n## P0.1. Initialize process artifacts when needed\\n\\n`BACKLOG.md` and `PLAN.md` are created before the first sprint. Progress artifacts, including `PROGRESS_BOARD.md` and sprint-specific records under `progress/`, are created and maintained by the RUP Strikes Back cycle when a sprint starts; do not create placeholder progress files in advance unless the Product Owner explicitly requests a pre-sprint artifact. Such a file does not start the sprint.\\n\\nBefore the first code-bearing sprint, define the project’s test profile in this patch or a referenced project rule. The project uses `docs/test-profile.md` for this purpose. It must map the generic test and regression fields to the actual project’s build, smoke-test, unit-test, integration-test, and applicable operational-validation commands. Do not claim that the submodule’s sample shell-test commands apply to the project. A test-profile template does not satisfy this gate: PBI-009 must replace its pending command fields with the candidate architecture's actual commands before Sprint 2 starts.\\n\\n## P0.2. Generic artifacts bootstrap Sprint 0\\n\\n`RUPStrikesBack.patch/bootstrap/` contains reusable RUP roadmap, backlog, sprint-plan, and test-profile templates, a generic `AGENTS.md`, and Codex skill adapters. `RUPStrikesBack.patch/BOOTSTRAP_PROMPT.md` is the operator-only bootstrap prompt and is deliberately kept outside the overlay. These artifacts are inputs to Sprint 0 only; they are not product requirements or active product backlog items. The bootstrap plan preselects Sprint 0 with `Status: Progress`, but execution still requires an explicit Product Owner request.\\n\\nAt project initialization, overlay the complete contents of `RUPStrikesBack.patch/bootstrap/` onto the project root, including hidden directories. Do not copy `RUPStrikesBack.patch/BOOTSTRAP_PROMPT.md` into the project root. The Product Owner explicitly requests execution of the preselected Sprint 0 and supplies the initial product intent through the working conversation. PBI-001 creates and accepts the project `README.md` as the vision product. Sprint 0 then derives this project’s real roadmap and proposes the next sprint from that vision. The generic later-sprint entries are initial hypotheses: Sprint 0 or subsequent iterations may retain, refine, split, reorder, or replace them. After Sprint 0, the root project artifacts—not the generic templates—are the sources of truth for the project’s scope and delivery work.\\n\\nThe generic bootstrap is deliberately tailored over its first three iterations. Sprint 0 proposes an initial lifecycle and iteration plan from the vision. Sprint 1 tests and refines that plan against business context, requirements, scope, risks, and an initial architecture. Sprint 2 refines it again from technical-validation evidence and the Lifecycle Architecture assessment. In each of these iterations, the agent must propose additions, removals, mergers, splits, reordered work, or a simpler path when the project’s size, uncertainty, existing assets, constraints, or risk profile justify it. In managed mode, the Product Owner accepts each material plan change. The plan must record the decision and rationale; generic tasks are defaults, never mandatory ceremony.\\n\\n## P0.3. Place project artifacts by lifecycle role\\n\\nThe root `README.md` holds the accepted product vision and project orientation. `docs/` holds durable shared project deliverables created and accepted during sprints, such as requirements, architecture, decisions, and operating documentation.\\n\\n`progress/<phase-ID>/` directories hold evidence and review artifacts for one sprint, including migrated historical evidence. `tmp/` holds provisional material retained only for reference; it is not a source of truth and must be recreated in `docs/` or another approved location when a sprint formally delivers it. `RUPStrikesBack.patch/bootstrap/` remains the reusable bootstrap input described above.\\n\\n## P0.4. Anchor progress-board items to an assigned sprint\\n\\n`PROGRESS_BOARD.md` follows the canonical four-column form and tracks both a\\nsprint's real-time status and the real-time statuses of backlog items assigned\\nto that sprint. Every backlog-item row must name the Sprint that contains the\\nitem in `PLAN.md`; an unscheduled item never receives a standalone progress\\nboard row.\\n\\nA Product Owner may promote an accepted review or feedback proposal into the\\nroot backlog with `Status: Proposed`. It remains there, without a progress\\nboard row, until the Product Owner prioritizes and assigns it to a named Sprint\\nin `PLAN.md`. The sprint procedure then creates its board row. This refines the\\ncanonical `/backlog add` instruction only for unscheduled proposals.\\n\\n## P1. Apply the RUP Strikes Back cycle proportionately\\n\\nThe Codex RUP Strikes Back manager's complete contracting, analysis/design, construction, quality-validation, and wrap-up pipeline is primarily a code-bearing Construction-iteration workflow. It should run fully when a sprint creates or materially changes production code.\\n\\nSprint 0 and Inception iterations use the same collaboration discipline, but produce vision, requirements, plans, risk evidence, and reviews rather than forcing implementation. An Elaboration iteration may construct a prototype and run technical tests when that is needed to retire risk, but it need not produce a production feature. In non-code-bearing iterations, construction and automated-test gates may be not applicable; the plan and sprint evidence mus…1863 tokens truncated…plete JDK, SQLcl, utPLSQL and Oracle workload on the reproducible E.17 baseline. Compare serial and concurrent execution to recommend runner capacity and caching with declared sample counts, measurement limits and unchanged mandatory validation.\\n\\nBacklog Items:\\n\\n* PBI-086. The improvement targets production and is not\\ncritical to Elaboration; earlier scheduling remains delegated to the\\nimplementor when justified, with the reason and revised assignment recorded\\nin this plan. worktrees\\n01a2190a-d633-7fe2-878a-23726aa58c72\\n# /sprint — Sprint Management\\n\\nManage sprints in `PLAN.md`.\\n\\n## Usage\\n\\n```\\n/sprint create [<N>] [--title <title>] [--items <ID1,ID2,...>]\\n/sprint status [<N>]\\n/sprint close [<N>]\\n/sprint start [<N>]\\n```\\n\\n## Operations\\n\\n### create\\n\\nCreate a new sprint entry in `PLAN.md`.\\n\\n**Steps:**\\n1. Read `PLAN.md` to find next sprint number (or use provided N)\\n2. Read `BACKLOG.md` to validate item IDs\\n3. Ask user for:\\n   - Title (short description of sprint goal)\\n   - Mode: `managed` or `YOLO`\\n   - Test levels: `smoke`, `unit`, `integration`, or `none`\\n   - Regression levels: `smoke`, `unit`, `integration`, or `none`\\n   - Backlog items to include (if not provided via --items)\\n4. Validate all required fields present\\n5. Append sprint to `PLAN.md`\\n6. Update `PROGRESS_BOARD.md` — add items with status \\\"Planned\\\"\\n\\n### status\\n\\nShow sprint status.\\n\\n**Steps:**\\n1. Read `PLAN.md`\\n2. If N provided, show that sprint; otherwise show active sprint (Status: Progress)\\n3. Display: status, mode, test/regression requirements, items, progress from PROGRESS_BOARD.md\\n\\n### start\\n\\nStart a sprint (transition from Planned to Progress).\\n\\n**Steps:**\\n1. Verify sprint exists and has Status: Planned\\n2. Update sprint status to \\\"Progress\\\" in `PLAN.md`\\n3. Update `PROGRESS_BOARD.md` — items to \\\"under_analysis\\\"\\n\\n### close\\n\\nComplete a sprint.\\n\\n**Steps:**\\n1. Verify all quality gates passed (check `progress/sprint_N/sprint_N_tests.md`)\\n2. Update sprint status to \\\"Done\\\" in `PLAN.md`\\n3. Update `PROGRESS_BOARD.md` — move items to \\\"Done\\\"\\n4. Optionally prompt for retrospective notes\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Format\\n\\nSee `rules/generic/sprint_definition.md` for full specification.\\n\\n```markdown\\n## Sprint <N> - <Title>\\n\\nStatus: Planned | Progress | Done\\nMode: managed | YOLO\\nTest: <smoke | unit | integration | none>\\nRegression: <smoke | unit | integration | none>\\n\\n<Optional: 1-2 sentences of context>\\n\\nBacklog Items:\\n\\n* <ID>. <Title>\\n```\\n\\n## Required Fields\\n\\n| Field | Values | Default |\\n|-------|--------|---------|\\n| Status | `Planned`, `Progress`, `Done` | `Planned` |\\n| Mode | `managed`, `YOLO` | `managed` |\\n| Test | `smoke`, `unit`, `integration`, `none` | `unit, integration` |\\n| Regression | `smoke`, `unit`, `integration`, `none` | `unit, integration` |\\n\\n## Validation\\n\\nBefore saving, verify:\\n\\n| Check | Rule |\\n|-------|------|\\n| Sprint N | Sequential, no gaps |\\n| Status | Valid value |\\n| Mode | Valid value |\\n| Test | Required, valid values |\\n| Regression | Required, valid values |\\n| Items exist | All IDs must exist in BACKLOG.md |\\n| No duplicates | Item cannot be in multiple active sprints |\\n\\n## Status Transitions\\n\\n```\\nPlanned → Progress → Done\\n                  → Failed\\n```\\n\\n- `Planned → Progress`: When `/sprint start` or `/rup-manager` begins\\n- `Progress → Done`: When `/sprint close` after all gates pass\\n- `Progress → Failed`: When quality gates fail after retries exhausted\\n# Sprint Definition\\n\\nA sprint entry in `PLAN.md` describes a unit of work the team commits to delivering. It must state what will be tested and what must not regress — so the implementor knows the quality bar before writing a line of code.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Format\\n\\n```markdown\\n## Sprint <N> - <Title>\\n\\nStatus: Planned | Progress | Done\\nMode: managed | YOLO\\nTest: <smoke | unit | integration | none>\\nRegression: <smoke | unit | integration | none>\\n\\n<Optional: 1-2 sentences of context if the sprint is non-obvious.>\\n\\nBacklog Items:\\n\\n* <ID>. <Title>\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Fields\\n\\n| Field | Required | Values | Description |\\n|-------|----------|--------|-------------|\\n| Status | Yes | `Planned`, `Progress`, `Done` | Current state of the sprint |\\n| Mode | Yes | `managed`, `YOLO` | `managed` = full RUP gates; `YOLO` = autonomous, minimal ceremony |\\n| Test | Yes | Comma-separated list | Which test levels to run for new code |\\n| Regression | Yes | Comma-separated list | Which existing tests to re-run after new-code gates pass |\\n\\n### Test Parameter Values\\n\\n| Value | Meaning |\\n|-------|---------|\\n| `smoke` | Quick critical checks in `tests/smoke/`. Fast gate to determine \\\"is it testable?\\\" |\\n| `unit` | Unit tests in `tests/unit/`. Tests individual functions. |\\n| `integration` | Integration tests in `tests/integration/`. End-to-end scenarios. |\\n| `none` | No new tests. For documentation-only or review-only sprints. |\\n\\n**Defaults if omitted:** `Test: unit, integration`\\n\\n### Regression Parameter Values\\n\\n| Value | Meaning |\\n|-------|---------|\\n| `smoke` | Re-run smoke tests only. Fastest regression. |\\n| `unit` | Re-run all unit tests (old + new). Good balance. |\\n| `integration` | Re-run all integration tests. Slowest but most thorough. |\\n| `none` | Skip regression. Only for experimental/throwaway sprints. |\\n\\n**Defaults if omitted:** `Regression: unit, integration`\\n\\n### Optional: Regression Scope\\n\\nLimit regression to specific component:\\n\\n```markdown\\nRegression scope: emit\\n```\\n\\nAvailable scopes map to `tests/manifests/component_<scope>.manifest`.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Constraints\\n\\n| Rule | Rationale |\\n|------|-----------|\\n| `Test` and `Regression` are required | They define exit criteria — no ambiguity |\\n| No design in sprint entry | Design belongs in elaboration document |\\n| No sub-tasks | Sub-tasks emerge during phases |\\n| No implementation notes | Implementation belongs in construction |\\n| One coherent deliverable | Bundle items only when tightly coupled |\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Status Transitions\\n\\n```\\nPlanned → Progress → Done\\n```\\n\\n| Transition | Trigger |\\n|------------|---------|\\n| `Planned → Progress` | When `/rup-cycle` or Phase 1 starts |\\n| `Progress → Done` | When all quality gates pass and Phase 5 completes |\\n| `Progress → Failed` | When retries exhausted and gates fail |\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Examples\\n\\n### Good Example\\n\\n```markdown\\n## Sprint 8 - curl backend for emit.sh\\n\\nStatus: Planned\\nMode: YOLO\\nTest: unit\\nRegression: unit\\n\\nBacklog Items:\\n\\n* SLI-11. Alternative HTTP backend for emit.sh\\n```\\n\\n**Why it's good:**\\n- Clear title\\n- All required fields present\\n- Test and Regression specify quality bar\\n- Single focused backlog item\\n\\n### Good Example — Full Suite\\n\\n```markdown\\n## Sprint 7 - Critical security fix\\n\\nStatus: Progress\\nMode: managed\\nTest: smoke, unit, integration\\nRegression: smoke, unit, integration\\n\\nSecurity vulnerability requires thorough testing at all levels.\\n\\nBacklog Items:\\n\\n* SEC-42. Fix authentication bypass in API gateway\\n```\\n\\n**Why it's good:**\\n- Context explains why full testing\\n- Mode is managed (human oversight for security)\\n- Maximum test coverage appropriate for risk\\n\\n### Good Example — Documentation Only\\n\\n```markdown\\n## Sprint 9 - API documentation update\\n\\nStatus: Planned\\nMode: YOLO\\nTest: none\\nRegression: smoke\\n\\nBacklog Items:\\n\\n* DOC-15. Update API reference for v2 endpoints\\n```\\n\\n**Why it's good:**\\n- `Test: none` is appropriate — no code changes\\n- `Regression: smoke` provides quick sanity check\\n- YOLO mode for documentation work\\n\\n### Bad Example — Missing Test and Regression\\n\\n```markdown\\n## Sprint 8 - curl backend for emit.sh\\n\\nStatus: Planned\\nMode: YOLO\\n\\nBacklog Items:\\n\\n* SLI-11. Split emit.sh into emit_oci.sh and emit_curl.sh\\n```\\n\\n**Why it's bad:**\\n- Missing `Test:` field — quality bar undefined\\n- Missing `Regression:` field — regression scope undefined\\n- Implementor doesn't know when they're done\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Validation Checklist\\n\\nBefore adding to PLAN.md, verify:\\n\\n- [ ] Sprint number is sequential (no gaps)\\n- [ ] Status is one of: Planned, Progress, Done\\n- [ ] Mode is one of: managed, YOLO\\n- [ ] Test field is present with valid values\\n- [ ] Regression field is present with valid values\\n- [ ] All backlog item IDs exist in BACKLOG.md\\n- [ ] No item appears in multiple active sprints\\n- [ ] Title is concise and descriptive\\n\"}"
      }
    ],

      "aggregated_output": "# RUP Cycle Manager - Complete Process Orchestration\n\nExecute the complete Rational Unified Process cycle with test-first quality gates.\n\n## Overview\n\nThis manager orchestrates all RUP phases by executing specialized agent instructions in sequence:\n\n| Phase | Agents | Output |\n|-------|--------|--------|\n| Phase 1: Setup | Contractor + Analyst | `sprint_N_setup.md` |\n| Phase 2: Design | Designer + Test Architect | `sprint_N_design.md` (includes test spec) |\n| Phase 3: Construction | Constructor | `sprint_N_implementation.md` |\n| Phase 4: Quality Gates | Test Executor | logs + `sprint_N_tests.md` |\n| Phase 5: Wrap-up | Documentor | README + traceability |\n\n## Instructions\n\nExecute all five phases in sequence. Each phase reads and executes its specialized agent's instructions. The manager handles transitions, git commits, and maintains flow continuity.\n\n---\n\n## Step 0: Detect Mode and Test Parameters (ONCE)\n\n**Before starting any phases, determine execution parameters:**\n\n1. **Read PLAN.md**\n2. **Identify the active Sprint** (Sprint with `Status: Progress`)\n3. **Extract parameters:**\n\n| Parameter | Values | Default |\n|-----------|--------|---------|\n| `Mode:` | `managed`, `YOLO` | `managed` |\n| `Test:` | `smoke`, `unit`, `integration`, `none` | `unit, integration` |\n| `Regression:` | `smoke`, `unit`, `integration`, `none` | `unit, integration` |\n| `Regression scope:` | component name | (full suite) |\n\nSee `rules/generic/sprint_definition.md` for parameter details.\n\n4. **Display Combined Banner:**\n\n```text\n═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════\nSPRINT [N] | MODE: [YOLO|managed] | Test: [values] | Regression: [values]\n═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════\n```\n\nIf **YOLO Mode**:\n```\nYOLO: All outputs self-approved, no waits, 10-min limit.\n- Agents make reasonable assumptions\n- All decisions logged in implementation docs\n- Critical failures still stop execution\n```\n\nIf **Managed Mode**:\n```\nMANAGED: Human-supervised at each phase.\n- Explicit approvals required\n- Agents ask for clarification\n- Recommended for complex/high-risk work\n```\n\n**All subsequent phases use this banner. Do NOT re-read PLAN.md for mode or sprint number.**\n\n---\n\n## YOLO Mode Speed Directive\n\n**TIME LIMIT:** Complete all 5 phases in MAX 10 minutes.\n\n**Speed Rules:**\n- Write MINIMAL documentation (bullets, not paragraphs)\n- Reference previous Sprints instead of re-explaining\n- Skip verbose examples - write code instead\n- Design: Endpoint list + data structures ONLY\n- Tests: Test table + pass/fail results ONLY\n- Max 3 YOLO decisions per phase (3 lines each)\n- NO redundant text, NO over-explanation\n\n---\n\n## Phase 1: Execute Setup (Contracting + Inception)\n\nRead `.claude/commands/agents/agent-contractor.md` and `.claude/commands/agents/agent-analyst.md` — skip Step 0 (mode detection) in both; mode is already set in the Step 0 banner.\n\nProduce a **single file** `progress/sprint_N/sprint_N_setup.md` with two sections:\n\n```markdown\n## Contract\n[contractor output: rules understood, responsibilities, constraints, open questions]\n\n## Analysis\n[analyst output: backlog items analyzed, feasibility, compatibility, open questions]\n```\n\nUpdate `PROGRESS_BOARD.md`: Sprint → `under_analysis`; Backlog Items → `analysed`\n\n**Decision Point:** If any critical ambiguity exists in either section, stop and request clarification.\n\n**Commit:** `docs(sprint-N): setup phase — contract and analysis` · Push.\n\n---\n\n## Phase 2: Execute Design + Test Specification\n\nRead `.claude/commands/agents/agent-designer.md` — skip Step 0 and Step 8 (\"Await Approval\").\n\n- **YOLO mode:** Self-approve immediately after writing; no wait.\n- **Managed mode:** Wait for explicit Product Owner approval before continuing.\n\nThe design document `progress/sprint_N/sprint_N_design.md` **MUST** include a `### Testing Strategy` section. See `rules/generic/testing_strategy_template.md` for the required format.\n\n**Immediately after writing the design** (no separate commit), execute Test Architect procedure from `rules/generic/test_procedures.md` Part 1:\n\n1. Read the `### Testing Strategy` section and the `Test:` param from the Step 0 banner\n2. Append a `## Test Specification` section to `sprint_N_design.md` (SM-N / UT-N / IT-N + traceability table)\n3. Append test skeletons to existing files in `tests/smoke/`, `tests/unit/`, `tests/integration/` (one file per component/domain, not per sprint)\n4. Register new tests in appropriate `tests/manifests/component_*.manifest`\n5. Write `progress/sprint_N/new_tests.manifest` (format: `suite:script[:function]`)\n6. Verify skeletons run and produce expected failures: `tests/run.sh --unit`\n\nUpdate `PROGRESS_BOARD.md`: Backlog Items → `designed` → `test_specified`\n\n**Decision Point:** If test scope is unclear after reading the Testing Strategy, stop and request clarification before writing skeletons.\n\n**Commit:** `docs(sprint-N): design, test spec, and test skeletons` · Push.\n\n---\n\n## Phase 3: Execute Construction\n\nRead `.claude/commands/agents/agent-constructor.md` — skip Step 0.\n\n**Override:** Do NOT create new test cases. Tests were already specified in Phase 2. The Constructor:\n\n1. Implements code to satisfy the design\n2. Fills in any `# TODO: implement` stubs in test skeletons\n3. Does NOT invent new test cases\n\nProduce `progress/sprint_N/sprint_N_implementation.md`.\n\nUpdate `PROGRESS_BOARD.md`: Sprint → `under_construction`; Backlog Items → `under_construction`\n\n**Commit:** `feat(sprint-N): implement [brief description]` · Push.\n\n**Do NOT proceed to Phase 5. Proceed to Phase 4.**\n\n---\n\n## Phase 4: Execute Quality Gates\n\nExecute Test Executor procedure from `rules/generic/test_procedures.md` Part 2. Use `Test:` and `Regression:` from the Step 0 banner.\n\n### Mandatory Log Artifacts\n\nEvery gate execution **MUST** produce a timestamped log file:\n\n```bash\nTS=\"$(date -u '+%Y%m%d_%H%M%S')\"\nLOG=\"progress/sprint_N/test_run_<gate>_${TS}.log\"\ntests/run.sh --<level> [flags] 2>&1 | tee \"$LOG\"\n```\n\nGate names: `A1_smoke`, `A2_unit`, `A3_integration`, `B1_smoke`, `B2_unit`, `B3_integration`\n\n### Phase A: New-Code Gates (per `Test:` parameter)\n\nRun only new tests from `new_tests.manifest`. Each must pass before the next:\n\n| Gate | Command | Condition |\n|------|---------|-----------|\n| A1 Smoke | `tests/run.sh --smoke --new-only progress/sprint_N/new_tests.manifest` | if `Test:` includes `smoke` |\n| A2 Unit | `tests/run.sh --unit --new-only progress/sprint_N/new_tests.manifest` | if `Test:` includes `unit` |\n| A3 Integration | `tests/run.sh --integration --new-only progress/sprint_N/new_tests.manifest` | if `Test:` includes `integration` |\n\nA1 fail = skip A2/A3 (build is too broken).\n\n### Phase B: Regression Gates (per `Regression:` parameter)\n\nRun after Phase A passes. Full suite unless `Regression scope:` is set:\n\n| Gate | Command | Condition |\n|------|---------|-----------|\n| B1 Smoke | `tests/run.sh --smoke [--component <scope>]` | if `Regression:` includes `smoke` |\n| B2 Unit | `tests/run.sh --unit [--component <scope>]` | if `Regression:` includes `unit` |\n| B3 Integration | `tests/run.sh --integration [--component <scope>]` | if `Regression:` includes `integration` |\n\n### Retry Policy\n\n| Mode | Retries 1-4 | Retry 5 | Retries 6-10 | After 10 |\n|------|-------------|---------|--------------|----------|\n| Managed | Auto fix-and-rerun | Human escalation | Continue if approved | Sprint `failed` |\n| YOLO | Auto fix-and-rerun | Auto | Auto | Sprint `failed` |\n\nSee `rules/generic/test_failure_classification.md` for flaky vs broken handling.\n\n### YOLO Mode Thresholds\n\n| Test Level | Required Pass Rate |\n|------------|-------------------|\n| Smoke | 100% (no exceptions) |\n| Unit | 100% (no exceptions) |\n| Integration | ≥80% with failures documented |\n\n### On Failure\n\n1. Hand failure report to Constructor (Phase 3)\n2. Constructor fixes the code\n3. Test Executor re-runs the failing gate\n4. Repeat until pass or retries exhausted\n\nProduce `progress/sprint_N/sprint_N_tests.md` with `## Artifacts` listing all log paths.\n\nUpdate `PROGRESS_BOARD.md`: Items → `smoke_passed` / `unit_tested` / `integration_tested` / `tested` (or `failed`); Sprint → `implemented` / `implemented_partially` / `failed`.\n\n**Commit:** `test(sprint-N): quality gates — [pass/fail summary]` · Push.\n\n**Phase 5 only after all gates pass (or YOLO threshold met). If retries exhausted → mark `failed`, still run Phase 5 for documentation.**\n\n---\n\n## Bug Handling\n\nBugs discovered during phases follow `rules/generic/bug_policy.md`:\n\n- **Fold-in:** Fix as part of current backlog item (default)\n- **Promote:** Create new backlog item if scope expands\n\nRegister bugs in `progress/sprint_N/sprint_N_bugs.md`.\n\n---\n\n## Phase 5: Execute Wrap-up\n\nRead `.claude/commands/agents/agent-documentor.md` and execute with these focuses:\n\n1. **Update `README.md`** — Add `### Sprint N — [title]` to Recent Updates section\n2. **Create backlog traceability Check for technology-specific development rules\n   - For GitHub Actions projects: `rules/github_actions/GitHub_DEV_RULES.md`\n   - For Ansible projects: `rules/ansible/` (future)\n   - For Terraform projects: `rules/terraform/` (future)\n\n### Step 3: Validate Understanding\n\nFor each document reviewed:\n- Summarize key points\n- Identify any unclear or conflicting requirements\n- Note any concerns or potential issues\n- Confirm compliance capability\n\n### Step 4: Enumerate Responsibilities\n\nList your responsibilities as an Implementor:\n- What you are allowed to edit\n- What you must never modify\n- How to propose changes\n- How to ask questions\n- Status tokens and state machines\n- Git commit requirements\n\n### Step 5: Document Understanding\n\nCreate a contracting summary that includes:\n\n1. **Project Overview**: Brief description of the project goals\n2. **Current Sprint**: Identify Sprint(s) in `Progress` status\n3. **Key Requirements**: Summary of active Backlog Items\n4. **Rule Compliance**: Confirmation of understanding for each rule document\n5. **Responsibilities**: Clear enumeration of Implementor role\n6. **Constraints**: List of prohibited actions\n7. **Communication Protocol**: How to propose changes and ask questions\n8. **Open Questions**: Any unclear points requiring clarification\n\n### Step 6: Create Contracting Summary\n\nOnce the contract is clear:\n\n- collect statistics about LLM tokens used for this phase to be added the phase summary\n- save your contracting summary `progress/sprint_${no}/sprint_${no}_contract.md`\n\n### Step 6: Finalize\n\n**If anything is unclear:**\n- Stop here\n- List all open questions\n- Wait for clarifications from Product Owner\n- Do NOT proceed to commit\n\n**If everything is clear:**\n- Confirm your readiness to proceed\n- State: \"Contracting phase complete - ready for Inception\"\n- Commit the contracting review file following semantic commit conventions\n- Use commit message format: `docs: sprint_${no} contracting phase completed`\n\n## Completion Criteria\n\nThe Contractor Agent has successfully completed when:\n\n- [x] All foundation documents read and understood\n- [x] All rule documents read and understood\n- [x] Responsibilities enumerated clearly\n- [x] Constraints and prohibited actions identified\n- [x] Communication protocols understood\n- [x] Open questions documented (if any)\n- [x] Contracting summary saved in proper location\n- [x] Changes committed with semantic commit message\n- [x] Readiness confirmed or clarifications requested\n- [x] LLM tokens statistics collected and save to file\n\n## Output Format\n\nYour final output should be:\n\n```markdown\n# Contracting Phase -
