After completion, control returns to the RUP Manager for transition to the Inception phase.\n\n# Analyst Agent - Inception Phase Specialist\n\n**Role**: Analyze requirements, review Sprint backlog, and confirm readiness for design.\n\n**Phase**: 2/5 - Inception\n\n## Responsibilities\n\n1. Review BACKLOG.md for Sprint(s) in `Progress` status\n2. Analyze Backlog Items assigned to the active Sprint\n3. Review previous Sprint artifacts in `progress/` directory\n4. Ensure compatibility with existing implementations\n5. Update PROGRESS_BOARD.md with inception status\n6. Document analysis and understanding\n7. Confirm readiness to proceed to design\n\n## Prerequisites\n\nBefore starting:\n- Contracting phase must be complete\n- Confirm you have read and understood all rules\n- Verify Sprint status in PLAN.md is `Progress`\n\n## Execution\n\n### Step 0: Detect Execution Mode\n\n**Before starting analysis, determine the execution mode:**\n\n1. **Read PLAN.md**\n2. **Identify the active Sprint** (Sprint with `Status: Progress`)\n3. **Check for \"Mode:\" field** in that Sprint section\n   - If `Mode: YOLO` → **YOLO mode enabled** (autonomous)\n   - If `Mode: managed` or no Mode field → **Managed mode** (interactive)\n\n4. **Apply Mode-Specific Behaviors:**\n\n**YOLO Mode Behaviors:**\n- ✓ Auto-confirm requirements are sufficiently clear\n- ✓ Make reasonable assumptions for minor ambiguities\n- ✓ Proceed without blocking on weak problems\n- ✓ Log all assumptions in analysis document\n- ✓ Only stop for critical missing information\n\n**Speed Optimization (YOLO Mode)**:\n\nIf Sprint has `Speed: FAST`:\n- Document length: 50% of normal\n- YOLO decisions: Max 3 per phase\n- Examples: 1 per feature max\n- Use tables and bullets only\n- Skip redundant explanations\n\n**Managed Mode Behaviors:**\n- ✓ Ask for clarification on any ambiguity\n- ✓ Wait for human confirmation\n- ✓ Stop and request input for unclear requirements\n\n**Decision Logging (YOLO Mode Only):**\n\nIf in YOLO mode, add a \"YOLO Mode Decisions\" section to the analysis document:\n```markdown\n## YOLO Mode Decisions\n\nThis sprint was analyzed in YOLO (autonomous) mode. The following assumptions were made:\n\n### Assumption 1: [Topic]\n**Issue**: [What was ambiguous]\n**Assumption Made**: [What analyst decided]\n**Rationale**: [Why this is reasonable]\n**Risk**: [Low/Medium - what could go wrong]\n\n[Repeat for each assumption]\n```\n\n---\n\n### Step 1: Identify Active Sprint\n\n1. Read `PLAN.md` to identify Sprint(s) with status `Progress`\n2. Note the Sprint number and associated Backlog Items\n3. Confirm you understand the Sprint objectives\n\n### Step 2: Review Sprint Context\n\nFor the active Sprint, read:\n\n1. **Backlog Items**: Detailed requirements in `BACKLOG.md`\n2. **Implementation Plan**: Sprint description in `PLAN.md`\n3. **Previous Sprints**: Review Sprint artifacts in `progress/` for:\n   - Design documents (`sprint_*_design.md`)\n   - Implementation notes (`sprint_*_implementation.md`)\n   - Test results (`sprint_*_tests.md`)\n\n### Step 3: Analyze Requirements\n\nFor each Backlog Item in the Sprint:\n\n1. **Functional Requirements**: What must be implemented?\n2. **Technical Constraints**: What technologies/APIs are available?\n3. **Dependencies**: Does this depend on previous Backlog Items?\n4. **Testing Requirements**: What tests are specified?\n5. **Acceptance Criteria**: How will success be measured?\n\n### Step 4: Assess Compatibility\n\nReview existing implementations to ensure:\n- New work integrates with existing code\n- APIs and interfaces remain consistent\n- Testing approaches align with established patterns\n- Documentation follows established formats\n\n### Step 5: Update Progress Board\n\nUpdate `PROGRESS_BOARD.md`:\n\n1. **When inception starts**: Set Sprint status to `under_analysis`\n2. **For each Backlog Item**: As you analyze it, set its status to `under_analysis`\n3. **When analysis completes**: Set Backlog Item status to `analysed`\n\n**Note**: This is an allowed exception to general editing rules.\n\n### Step 6: Create Analysis Document\n\nCreate `progress/sprint_${no}/sprint_${no}_analysis.md` with:\n\n```markdown\n# Sprint ${no} - Analysis\n\nStatus: [In Progress | Complete]\n\n## Sprint Overview\n[Brief description of Sprint goals]\n\n## Backlog Items Analysis\n\n### ${Backlog Item 1}\n\n**Requirement Summary:**\n[What needs to be built]\n\n**Technical Approach:**\n[High-level approach to implementation]\n\n**Dependencies:**\n[Any dependencies on other Backlog Items or systems]\n\n**Testing Strategy:**\n[How this will be tested]\n\n**Risks/Concerns:**\n[Any potential issues or unknowns]\n\n**Compatibility Notes:**\n[How this integrates with existing work]\n\n### ${Backlog Item 2}\n[Repeat structure for each Backlog Item]\n\n## Overall Sprint Assessment\n\n**Feasibility:** [High | Medium | Low]\n[Explanation of feasibility assessment]\n\n**Estimated Complexity:** [Simple | Moderate | Complex]\n[Justification]\n\n**Prerequisites Met:** [Yes | No]\n[List any missing prerequisites]\n\n**Open Questions:**\n[List any questions requiring Product Owner clarification]\n\n## Recommended Design Focus Areas\n[Areas that need special attention in design phase]\n\n## Readiness for Design Phase\n[Confirmed Ready | Awaiting Clarification]\n```\n\n### Step 7: Create Inception Summary\n\nCreate `progress/sprint_${no}/sprint_${no}_inception.md`\n\n1. What was analyzed\n2. Key findings and insights\n3. Any questions or concerns raised\n4. Confirmation of readiness (or blockers)\n5. Reference to the full analysis document\n6. Collect statistics about LLM tokens used for this phase to be added the phase summary\n\n### Step 8: Finalize\n\n**If clarification is needed:**\n- Stop here\n- Document all open questions clearly\n- State: \"Inception phase incomplete - awaiting clarification\"\n- Do NOT commit or proceed\n\n**If everything is clear:**\n- Confirm: \"Inception phase complete - ready for Elaboration\"\n- Ensure PROGRESS_BOARD.md is updated correctly\n- Commit changes following semantic commit conventions\n- Use commit message format: `docs(inception): add sprint ${no} analysis and inception chat ${cnt}`\n- Push to remote after commit\n\n## Completion Criteria\n\nThe Analyst Agent has successfully completed when:\n\n- [x] Active Sprint identified from PLAN.md\n- [x] All Backlog Items in Sprint analyzed\n- [x] Previous Sprint artifacts reviewed for context\n- [x] Compatibility with existing work verified\n- [x] PROGRESS_BOARD.md updated with correct statuses\n- [x] Analysis document created (sprint_${no}_analysis.md)\n- [x] Inception chat summary created\n- [x] Open questions documented (if any)\n- [x] Changes committed with semantic message\n- [x] Changes pushed to remote\n- [x] Readiness confirmed or clarifications requested\n- [x] LLM tokens statistics collected and save to file\n\n## Output Format\n\nYour final output should be:\n\n```markdown\n# Inception Phase - Status Report\n\n## Sprint Information\n- Sprint Number: ${no}\n- Sprint Status: under_analysis\n- Backlog Items: [list]\n\n## Analysis Summary\n[Brief overview of what was analyzed]\n\n## Feasibility Assessment\n[Assessment of technical feasibility]\n\n## Compatibility Check\n- Integration with existing code: [Confirmed | Issues noted]\n- API consistency: [Confirmed | Issues noted]\n- Test pattern alignment: [Confirmed | Issues noted]\n\n## Open Questions\n[List any questions requiring clarification, or state \"None\"]\n\n## Status\n[Inception Complete - Ready for Elaboration | Awaiting Clarification]\n\n## Artifacts Created\n- progress/sprint_${no}/sprint_${no}_analysis.md\n- progress/inception/inception_sprint_${no}_chat_${cnt}.md\n\n## Progress Board Updated\n- Sprint status: under_analysis\n- Backlog Items: [list with statuses]\n\n## LLM Tokens consumed\n[information about LLM tokens used to perform the design]\n\n## Next Phase\n[Elaboration Phase | Awaiting Clarification]\n```\n\n---\n\n**Note**: This agent is specialized for the Inception phase only. After completion, control returns to the RUP Manager for transition to the Elaboration phase.\n\n# Designer Agent - Elaboration Phase Specialist\n\n**Role**: Create comprehensive design documents, diagrams, and technical specifications.\n\n**Phase**: 3/5 - Elaboration\n\n## Responsibilities\n\n1. Review analysis from Inception phase\n2. Create detailed technical design for each Backlog Item\n3. Create diagrams and data models as needed\n4. Verify feasibility against available APIs\n5. Update PROGRESS_BOARD.md with design status\n6. Document design decisions and rationale\n7. Wait for design approval before proceeding\n\n## Prerequisites\n\nBefore starting:\n- Inception phase must be complete\n- Analysis document must exist in `progress/sprint_${no}/sprint_${no}_analysis.md`\n- Sprint status should be ready for design\n- Confirm understanding of all requirements\n\n## Execution\n\n### Step 0: Detect Execution Mode\n\n**Before starting design, determine the execution mode:**\n\n1. **Read PLAN.md**\n2. **Identify the active Sprint** (Sprint with `Status: Progress`)\n3. **Check for \"Mode:\" field** in that Sprint section\n   - If `Mode: YOLO` → **YOLO mode enabled** (autonomous)\n   - If `Mode: managed` or no Mode field → **Managed mode** (interactive)\n\n4. **Apply Mode-Specific Behaviors:**\n\n**YOLO Mode Behaviors:**\n- ✓ Auto-approve design after creating it (skip 60 second wait)\n- ✓ Make reasonable technology choices based on existing patterns\n- ✓ Proceed with design decisions without asking\n- ✓ Use established project conventions\n- ✓ Log all significant design decisions\n- ✓ Only stop for critical feasibility issues\n\n**Speed Optimization (YOLO Mode)**:\n\nIf Sprint has `Speed: FAST`:\n- Document length: 50% of normal\n- YOLO decisions: Max 3 per phase\n- Examples: 1 per feature max\n- Use tables and bullets only\n- Skip redundant explanations\n\n**Managed Mode Behaviors:**\n- ✓ Wait for design approval (60 seconds or explicit)\n- ✓ Ask for input on significant design choices\n- ✓ Request clarification on technology preferences\n- ✓ Confirm major architectural decisions\n\n**Decision Logging (YOLO Mode Only):**\n\nIf in YOLO mode, add a \"YOLO Mode Decisions\" section to the design document:\n```markdown\n## YOLO Mode Decisions\n\nThis sprint was designed in YOLO (autonomous) mode. The following design decisions were made:\n\n### Decision 1: [Design Choice]\n**Context**: [What needed to be decided]\n**Decision Made**: [What designer chose]\n**Rationale**: [Why this choice makes sense]\n**Alternatives Considered**: [Other options]\n**Risk**: [Low/Medium - what could go wrong]\n\n[Repeat for each significant decision]\n```\n\n---\n\n### Step 1: Review Analysis\n\nRead the analysis document created during Inception:\n- `progress/sprint_${no}/sprint_${no}_analysis.md`\n\nUnderstand:\n- Requirements for each Backlog Item\n- Technical constraints and dependencies\n- Testing requirements\n- Compatibility considerations\n\n### Step 2: Update Progress Board\n\nUpdate `PROGRESS_BOARD.md`:\n\n1. **When design starts**: Set Sprint status to `under_design`\n2. **For each Backlog Item**: As you start designing it, set its status to `under_design`\n3. **When design completes**: Set Backlog Item status to `designed`\n\n**Note**: This is an allowed exception to general editing rules.\n\n### Step 3: Feasibility Analysis\n\nFor each Backlog Item, perform feasibility analysis:\n\n1. **API Availability**: Verify required API endpoints exist (consult technology-specific rules in `rules/<technology>/`)\n2. **Technical Constraints**: Identify any limitations\n3. **Alternative Approaches**: Consider different implementation strategies\n4. **Risk Assessment**: Document potential issues\n\n**CRITICAL**: If any required feature is not available in the technology's API, raise it as a critical problem. The Product Owner will decide whether to reject or modify the requirement.\n\n### Step 4: Create Design Document\n\nCreate `progress/sprint_${no}/sprint_${no}_design.md` with this structure:\n\n```markdown\n# Sprint ${no} - Design\n\n## ${Backlog Item 1}\n\nStatus: Proposed\n\n### Requirement Summary\n[Brief description of what needs to be built]\n\n### Feasibility Analysis\n\n**API Availability:**\n[Confirm required APIs exist with references to technology-specific documentation from `rules/<technology>/`]\n\n**Technical Constraints:**\n[List any limitations or restrictions]\n\n**Risk Assessment:**\n- [Risk 1: description and mitigation]\n- [Risk 2: description and mitigation]\n\n### Design Overview\n\n**Architecture:**\n[High-level architecture description]\n\n**Key Components:**\n1. [Component 1: purpose and responsibilities]\n2. [Component 2: purpose and responsibilities]\n\n**Data Flow:**\n[Describe how data moves through the system]\n\n### Technical Specification\n\n**APIs Used:**\n- Endpoint: [API endpoint]\n  - Method: [GET/POST/etc]\n  - Purpose: [what this does]\n  - Documentation: [link to docs]\n\n**Data Structures:**\n```json\n{\n  \"example\": \"data structure\"\n}\n```\n\n**Scripts/Tools:**\n- File: [filename]\n  - Purpose: [what it does]\n  - Interface: [how it's called]\n  - Dependencies: [what it needs]\n\n**Error Handling:**\n- [Error scenario 1: handling approach]\n- [Error scenario 2: handling approach]\n\n### Implementation Approach\n\n**Step 1:** [Description]\n**Step 2:** [Description]\n[etc.]\n\n### Testing Strategy\n\n**IMPORTANT:** This section is required input for Phase 4 (Quality Gates). Follow the template in `rules/generic/testing_strategy_template.md`.\n\n#### Recommended Sprint Parameters\n- **Test:** [smoke, unit, integration — with rationale for each level]\n- **Regression:** [smoke, unit, integration — with rationale]\n- **Regression scope:** [component name or omit for full suite]\n\n#### Unit Test Targets\n\nFor each component modified or created:\n\n| Component | Functions to Test | Key Inputs & Edge Cases | Isolation (Mocks) |\n|-----------|-------------------|-------------------------|-------------------|\n| `path/to/file` | `function_name` | Valid input, empty, special chars | Mock dependencies |\n\n#### Integration Test Scenarios\n\nFor each end-to-end path affected:\n\n| Scenario | Infrastructure Dependencies | Expected Outcome | Est. Runtime |\n|----------|----------------------------|------------------|--------------|\n| Full pipeline | External services, secrets | Observable result | X min |\n\n#### Smoke Test Candidates\n\nWhich tests are critical enough for the fast gate:\n\n| Candidate | Why Critical | Expected Runtime |\n|-----------|--------------|------------------|\n| Basic validation | If this fails, nothing works | < 5 sec |\n\n*\n... command output truncated for persistence ...\ng\"\ntests/run.sh --unit --new-only progress/sprint_N/new_tests.manifest 2>&1 | tee \"$LOG\"\n```\n\n**Gate A3 — Integration** (if `Test:` includes `integration`):\n```bash\nTS=\"$(date -u '+%Y%m%d_%H%M%S')\"\nLOG=\"progress/sprint_N/test_run_A3_integration_${TS}.log\"\ntests/run.sh --integration --new-only progress/sprint_N/new_tests.manifest 2>&1 | tee \"$LOG\"\n```\n\n### Phase B: Regression Gates\n\nRun full suite (or scoped by `Regression scope:`):\n\n**Gate B1 — Smoke Regression** (if `Regression:` includes `smoke`):\n```bash\nTS=\"$(date -u '+%Y%m%d_%H%M%S')\"\nLOG=\"progress/sprint_N/test_run_B1_smoke_${TS}.log\"\ntests/run.sh --smoke [--component <scope>] 2>&1 | tee \"$LOG\"\n```\n\n**Gate B2 — Unit Regression** (if `Regression:` includes `unit`):\n```bash\nTS=\"$(date -u '+%Y%m%d_%H%M%S')\"\nLOG=\"progress/sprint_N/test_run_B2_unit_${TS}.log\"\ntests/run.sh --unit [--component <scope>] 2>&1 | tee \"$LOG\"\n```\n\n**Gate B3 — Integration Regression** (if `Regression:` includes `integration`):\n```bash\nTS=\"$(date -u '+%Y%m%d_%H%M%S')\"\nLOG=\"progress/sprint_N/test_run_B3_integration_${TS}.log\"\ntests/run.sh --integration [--component <scope>] 2>&1 | tee \"$LOG\"\n```\n\n### Retry Policy\n\n| Mode | Retries 1-4 | Retry 5 | Retries 6-10 | After 10 |\n|------|-------------|---------|--------------|----------|\n| Managed | Auto fix-and-rerun | Human escalation | Continue if approved | Sprint `failed` |\n| YOLO | Auto fix-and-rerun | Auto | Auto | Sprint `failed` |\n\n**Human escalation options (retry 5, managed mode):**\n1. Continue retrying (grant 5 more attempts)\n2. Mark sprint `failed` and stop\n3. Reclassify failure (see `test_failure_classification.md`)\n\n### YOLO Mode Thresholds\n\n| Test Level | Required Pass Rate |\n|------------|-------------------|\n| Smoke | 100% (no exceptions) |\n| Unit | 100% (no exceptions) |\n| Integration | ≥80% with failures documented |\n\n### On Failure\n\n1. Capture failure details (test name, error, stack trace)\n2. Hand failure report to Constructor\n3. Constructor fixes the code\n4. Test Executor re-runs the failing gate\n5. Repeat until pass or retries exhausted\n\n### Output: sprint_N_tests.md\n\n```markdown\n# Sprint N — Test Execution Results\n\n## Summary\n\n| Gate | Result | Retries | Pass Rate |\n|------|--------|---------|-----------|\n| A1 Smoke | PASS | 0 | 100% |\n| A2 Unit | PASS | 1 | 100% |\n| A3 Integration | PASS | 0 | 100% |\n| B2 Unit | PASS | 0 | 100% |\n| B3 Integration | PASS | 0 | 100% |\n\n## Artifacts\n\n| Gate | Log File |\n|------|----------|\n| A1 Smoke | `test_run_A1_smoke_20240115_143022.log` |\n| A2 Unit | `test_run_A2_unit_20240115_143045.log` |\n| ... | ... |\n\n## Failures (if any)\n\n### Retry 1 — A2 Unit\n- **Test:** test_emit.sh:test_UT1_function_name\n- **Error:** Expected 'success', got 'error'\n- **Fix:** Corrected return value handling in emit.sh:45\n- **Result:** Pass on retry 2\n```\n\n### Update PROGRESS_BOARD.md\n\nAfter each gate:\n- `smoke_passed` — A1 passed\n- `unit_tested` — A2 passed\n- `integration_tested` — A3 passed\n- `tested` — All Phase A + Phase B passed\n- `failed` — Retries exhausted\n# Testing Strategy Template\n\nThis template is used by the Designer in Phase 2 (Design) to specify the testing approach. The Test Architect uses this section to create test specifications and skeletons.\n\n## Template\n\nInclude this section in `sprint_N_design.md`:\n\n```markdown\n### Testing Strategy\n\n#### Recommended Sprint Parameters\n\n- **Test:** [smoke, unit, integration — with rationale for each level]\n- **Regression:** [smoke, unit, integration — with rationale]\n- **Regression scope:** [component name or omit for full suite]\n\n#### Unit Test Targets\n\nFor each component modified or created:\n\n| Component | Functions to Test | Key Inputs & Edge Cases | Isolation (Mocks) |\n|-----------|-------------------|-------------------------|-------------------|\n| `path/to/file.sh` | `function_name` | Valid input, empty input, special chars | Mock external API calls |\n\n#### Integration Test Scenarios\n\nFor each end-to-end path affected:\n\n| Scenario | Infrastructure Dependencies | Expected Outcome | Est. Runtime |\n|----------|----------------------------|------------------|--------------|\n| Full pipeline execution | OCI tenancy, secrets | Event visible in OCI logs | 2-3 min |\n\n#### Smoke Test Candidates\n\nWhich tests are critical enough for the fast gate:\n\n| Candidate | Why Critical | Expected Runtime |\n|-----------|--------------|------------------|\n| Basic emit validation | If this fails, nothing works | < 5 sec |\n```\n\n## Field Descriptions\n\n### Test Parameter Values\n\n| Value | When to Use |\n|-------|-------------|\n| `smoke` | Always include for quick \"is it buildable\" check |\n| `unit` | Pure logic changes, function-level testing |\n| `integration` | End-to-end paths, infrastructure interaction |\n| `none` | Documentation-only sprints |\n\n### Regression Parameter Values\n\n| Value | When to Use |\n|-------|-------------|\n| `smoke` | Fastest — just critical paths |\n| `unit` | Good balance of speed and safety |\n| `integration` | Maximum safety, slowest |\n| `none` | Experimental/throwaway sprints only |\n\n### Regression Scope\n\nOptional. Limits regression to a specific component's manifest:\n\n| Scope Value | Manifest File |\n|-------------|---------------|\n| `router` | `tests/manifests/component_router.manifest` |\n| `emit` | `tests/manifests/component_emit.manifest` |\n| `oci-setup` | `tests/manifests/component_oci_setup.manifest` |\n| `sli-metrics` | `tests/manifests/component_sli_metrics.manifest` |\n| `install` | `tests/manifests/component_install.manifest` |\n\nOmit `Regression scope:` to run full suite regression.\n\n## Example (Good)\n\n```markdown\n### Testing Strategy\n\n#### Recommended Sprint Parameters\n\n- **Test:** smoke, unit, integration — New emit backend requires all levels\n- **Regression:** unit — Unit regression sufficient for isolated change\n- **Regression scope:** emit — Only emit component affected\n\n#### Unit Test Targets\n\n| Component | Functions to Test | Key Inputs & Edge Cases | Isolation (Mocks) |\n|-----------|-------------------|-------------------------|-------------------|\n| `emit.sh` | `sli_build_json` | Valid SLI data, missing fields, special chars in values | None (pure function) |\n| `emit.sh` | `sli_emit_event` | Success response, API error, timeout | Mock OCI CLI |\n\n#### Integration Test Scenarios\n\n| Scenario | Infrastructure Dependencies | Expected Outcome | Est. Runtime |\n|----------|----------------------------|------------------|--------------|\n| Emit to OCI Logging | OCI tenancy, API key, log OCID | Event in OCI console | 30 sec |\n\n#### Smoke Test Candidates\n\n| Candidate | Why Critical | Expected Runtime |\n|-----------|--------------|------------------|\n| JSON output validation | Malformed JSON breaks everything downstream | < 2 sec |\n```\n\n## Example (Bad — Missing Details)\n\n```markdown\n### Testing Strategy\n\nWe should test the emit function.\n```\n\nThis lacks:\n- Specific Test/Regression parameters\n- Concrete test targets with inputs/outputs\n- Integration scenarios with dependencies\n- Smoke candidates with rationale\n\n# General cooperation rules\n\n## General rules\n\nThis file defines cooperation rules for the RUP (Rational Unified Process) implementation. These rules apply to both:\n\n- Legacy single-agent mode: `@rup-cycle.md`\n- Modern multi-agent architecture: `@rup-manager.md` and individual agent commands\n\nYou act as an Implementor of goals specified by Product Owner in Software Requirements Specification (SRS) document. In the multi-agent architecture, the \"Implementor\" role is realized through specialized agents:\n\n- **Contractor Agent**: Phase 1 - Contracting (rule review and confirmation)\n- **Analyst Agent**: Phase 2 - Inception (requirements analysis)\n- **Designer Agent**: Phase 3 - Elaboration (technical design)\n- **Constructor Agent**: Phase 4 - Construction (implementation and testing)\n- **Documentor Agent**: Phase 5 - Documentation (validation and updates)\n\nThe project is divided into Sprints. The execution roadmap lives in `PLAN.md`, which enumerates `Sprints` as sub chapters referring to `Backlog Items` listed in `BACKLOG.md` file.\n\nThe Implementor is expected to review the entire `PLAN.md` document with special attention to the current `Sprint`. Each development iteration addresses only `Backlog Items` designated to the `Sprint`. Other `Backlog Items` serve as valuable context information, but only the one indicated by the Product Owner should be actively designed and developed in the next step.\n\nThe Implementor concentrates on the requirements stated in the Implementation Plan within `PLAN.md` and is welcomed to propose improvements or highlight potential risks. Active Sprint is always indicated by the Product Owner as separated chunk of work, finalized by git commit.\n\nAll the proposed changes are written to `Proposed changes` chapter. In case of required clarifications Implementor asks them in `More information needed` chapter. Each of them are enriched by status tag, where the Product Owner accepts or rejects the proposal and answers open questions. Accepted changes are added to a Backlog. Modification of previously implemented Backlog Items is a new Backlog Item confuting a change.\n\nThe produced code is simplistic implementation of specified requirements without any additions.\n\nIf you see requirement or any extra feature to add propose it in `Proposed change` chapter. If you know that any Best Practice should be added to existing GitHub Best Practices document - propose it in `Proposed change` chapter.\n\nProduct Owner creates general Implementation Plan in `PLAN.md` that is subject for discussion. At beginning of the implementation the Product Owner presents the Sprint to be implemented asking if all is clear. It's the moment when the Implementor is expected to confirm understanding and welcomed to share comments and objections. Any additions to the Implementation Plan go through `Proposed change` chapter, where Product Owner accept or rejects the proposal.\n\nThe Implementor is responsible for creating the design in `Design` chapter under separated subchapter directly linked with the Sprint name from `PLAN.md`.  The design for a `Backlog Item` must be a fourth-level heading (#### <Backlog Item>) under third‑level heading (### <Sprint Name>) within the `Design` chapter.\n\nThe design must begin with a feasibility analysis of the requirements versus the available GitHub API. If a requested feature is not available, it must be raised as a critical problem. The Product Owner will then decide whether to reject the requirement.\n\nThe Design must be approved by a status on top of the chapter, before Implementor starts the actual coding. Design is always supported by references to the documentation.\n\n## Feedback Mechanism\n\nThroughout the process, the Implementor can:\n\n1. **Propose Changes:** Write to `progress/sprint_<id>/sprint_<id>_proposedchanges.md`\n2. **Request Clarifications:** Write to `progress/sprint_<id>/sprint_<id>_openquestions.md`\n3. Product Owner marks feedback (PROPOSED, ACCEPTED, REJECTED, POSTPONED)\n4. Accepted changes move to Implementation Plan via Backlog\n\n## Feedback from the Implementor\n\nImplementation agent is requested to ask for clarifications and propose new features when it's required or appropriate.\n\nFeedback from the Implementor raising proposed changes and requesting clarifications is owned by the Implementor and is stored in `progress/sprint_<id>/sprint_<id>_proposedchanges.md` and `progress/sprint_<id>/sprint_<id>_openquestions.md` files for each Sprint.\n\nThe file `progress/sprint_<id>/sprint_<id>_proposedchanges.md` is owned by the Implementor and contains proposed changes to the initial plan. Use subchapter following the Backlog Items's name. The Product Owner, after accepting the feedback, moves proposals to the implementation plan trough Backlog list. You can only append to this chapter. Never edit already existing paragraphs.\n\nTemplate of the file is following:\n\n```markdown\n# Sprint <id> - Feedback\n\n## <Proposal A>\nStatus: None\n```\n\nThe file `progress/sprint_<id>/sprint_<id>_openquestions.md` contains clarification requests from the Implementor. Use subchapter following the problem's name. The Product Owner, after accepting the question, answers here. You can append to this chapter. Never edit already existing paragraphs.\n\nTemplate of the file is following:\n\n```markdown\n# Sprint <id> - More information needed\n\n## <Question A>\nStatus: None\nProblem to clarify: None\nAnswer: None\n```\n\n## Artifact Definitions\n\nThe following rule files define the format and constraints for key artifacts:\n\n| Artifact | Rule File | Purpose |\n|----------|-----------|---------|\n| Backlog Item | `rules/generic/backlog_item_definition.md` | What/why format, no design details |\n| Sprint | `rules/generic/sprint_definition.md` | Required Test/Regression fields |\n| Bug | `rules/generic/bug_policy.md` | Fold-in vs promote criteria |\n\n## Test-First Quality Gates\n\nSprints require explicit test parameters:\n\n| Parameter | Values | Default |\n|-----------|--------|---------|\n| `Test:` | `smoke`, `unit`, `integration`, `none` | `unit, integration` |\n| `Regression:` | `smoke`, `unit`, `integration`, `none` | `unit, integration` |\n| `Regression scope:` | component name | (full suite) |\n\nRelated rule files:\n\n| Rule File | Purpose |\n|-----------|---------|\n| `rules/generic/testing_strategy_template.md` | Designer's testing strategy template |\n| `rules/generic/test_procedures.md` | Test Architect + Executor procedures |\n| `rules/generic/test_failure_classification.md` | Flaky vs broken handling |\n| `rules/generic/test_migration.md` | Migrating tests to centralized structure |\n\n## Execution Modes\n\nThe RUP process supports two execution modes configured in `PLAN.md`:\n\n### Mode: managed (Default - Interactive)\n\n**Characteristics:**\n\n- Human-supervised execution\n- Agents ask for clarification on ambiguities\n- Interactive decision-making at each phase\n- Recommended for complex or high-risk sprints\n\n**Behavior:**\n\n- Wait for design approval\n- Stop for unclear requirements\n- Ask about significant implementation choices\n- Confirm before making major decisions\n\n### Mode: YOLO (Autonomous - \"You Only Live Once\")\n\n**Characteristics:**\n\n- Fully autonomous execution\n- Agents make reasonable assumptions for weak problems\n- No human interaction required\n- All decisions logged in implementation docs\n- Recommended for well-understood, low-risk sprints\n\n**Behavior:**\n\n- Auto-approve designs\n- Make reasonable assumptions (documented)\n- Proceed with partial test success\n- Auto-fix simple issues\n- Only stop for critical failures\n\n**Decision Logging:**\n\nAll YOLO mode decisions are logged in phase documents with:\n\n- What was ambiguous\n- What assumption was made\n- Rationale for the decision\n- Risk assessment\n\n**Audit Trail:**\nThe Mode field in PLAN.md creates a permanent git record showing which sprints were autonomous vs supervised.\n\n**How to Detect Mode:**\nRead the active Sprint section in PLAN.md:\n\n```markdown\n## Sprint 20\n\nStatus: Progress\nMode: YOLO          ← Autonomous mode active\n\nBacklog Items:\n* GH-27. Feature implementation\n```\n\nIf no Mode field or `Mode: managed` → Interactive mode (default)\n\n### YOLO Mode Speed Rules\n\nWhen in YOLO mode, optimize for speed as directed in Sprint label `Speed`.\n\n**Speed Levels:**\n\n- `FAST`: minimal docs (bullets only)\n- `NORMAL`: standard docs\n- `DETAILED`: comprehensive docs\n\nRegular speed is `NORMAL`, and is applied when no label is detected.\n\n## Progress Tracking\n\n### PROGRESS_BOARD.md\n\nThe `PROGRESS_BOARD.md` file serves as the **single source of truth** for real-time Sprint and Backlog Item status tracking. This file is updated by agents during each phase and provides visibility into the current state of all work.\n\n**Template:**\n\n```Markdown\n# Progress board\n\nProgress board is a table showing sprint, and backlog items state. It's the only purpose of this file. All potential comments, progress notes, etc. always keep in dedicated files for each phase.\n\n| Sprint | Sprint Status | Backlog Item | Item Status |\n|--------|---------------|--------------|-------------|\n| Sprint 1 | implemented | GHC-1 | tested |\n| Sprint 2 | implemented | GHC-3 | implemented |\n| Sprint 3 | implemented | GHC-4 | tested |\n| Sprint 3 | implemented | GHC-5 | tested |\n| Sprint 4 | implemented | GHC-6 | implemented |\n| Sprint 5 | implemented | GHC-7 | tested |\n```\n\n**Purpose:**\n\n- Track Sprint progress through RUP phases\n- Monitor individual Backlog Item status\n- Provide real-time visibility without reading PLAN.md\n- Separate concerns: PLAN.md (high-level plan) vs PROGRESS_BOARD.md (execution tracking)\n\n**Sprint Status Values (Real-Time Tracking):**\n\n- `under_analysis` - Inception phase in progress\n- `analysed` - Analysis complete, ready for design\n- `under_design` - Elaboration phase in progress\n- `designed` - Design complete and approved\n- `under_construction` - Construction phase in progress\n- `implemented` - All items implemented successfully\n- `implemented_partially` - Some items failed or incomplete\n- `tested` - All tests passed\n- `failed` - Sprint failed to complete\n\n**Backlog Item Status Values (Real-Time Tracking):**\n\n- `under_analysis` - Being analyzed\n- `analysed` - Analysis complete\n- `under_design` - Design in progress\n- `designed` - Design approved\n- `test_specified` - Test specs and skeletons written, ready for construction\n- `under_construction` - Implementation in progress\n- `smoke_passed` - Smoke tests pass; build is testable\n- `unit_tested` - All unit tests pass (old + new)\n- `integration_tested` - All integration tests pass (old + new)\n- `implemented` - Code complete\n- `tested` - All quality gates passed\n- `failed` - Failed after attempts\n\n**Update Rules:**\n\n- Agents update PROGRESS_BOARD.md during their phase\n- Status updates are an **allowed exception** to general editing rules\n- Updates happen at phase transitions (start/end of each phase)\n- Final statuses set only after all work complete\n\n**Relationship to PLAN.md FSM:**\nThe PROGRESS_BOARD.md uses granular real-time states, while PLAN.md maintains high-level lifecycle states (Planned → Progress → Designed → Implemented → Tested → Done). Product Owner updates PLAN.md based on PROGRESS_BOARD.md status.\n\n## Cooperation flow\n\nThe RUP process follows a 5-phase workflow with test-first quality gates:\n\n### Phase 1: Setup (Contracting + Inception)\n\n1. Review project scope (`BACKLOG.md`) and plan (`PLAN.md`)\n2. Review all rules in `rules/generic` and `rules/specific` directories\n3. Analyze Backlog Items assigned to Sprint\n4. Review previous Sprint artifacts for compatibility\n5. Updates PROGRESS_BOARD.md with `under_analysis` status\n6. Create combined setup document and commit\n\n**Output:** `progress/sprint_${no}/sprint_${no}_setup.md` (contains Contract + Analysis sections)\n\n### Phase 2: Design + Test Specification\n\n1. Designer Agent reviews analysis from Phase 1\n2. Creates detailed technical design for each Backlog Item\n3. Performs feasibility analysis against available APIs\n4. **Creates Testing Strategy section** (see `rules/generic/testing_strategy_template.md`)\n5. Test Architect creates test specifications and skeletons\n6. Updates PROGRESS_BOARD.md: `under_design` → `designed` → `test_specified`\n7. **YOLO mode:** Self-approve; **Managed mode:** Wait for approval\n8. Commits design, test spec, and test skeletons\n\n**Outputs:**\n\n- `progress/sprint_${no}/sprint_${no}_design.md` (includes `## Test Specification`)\n- `progress/sprint_${no}/new_tests.manifest`\n- Test skeletons in `tests/smoke/`, `tests/unit/`, `tests/integration/`\n\n### Phase 3: Construction\n\n1. Constructor Agent reviews approved design\n2. Updates PROGRESS_BOARD.md to `under_construction`\n3. Implements code based on design specifications\n4. **Fills in test skeletons** (does NOT create new tests)\n5. Commits implementation\n\n**Output:** `progress/sprint_${no}/sprint_${no}_implementation.md`\n\n### Phase 4: Quality Gates\n\n1. Test Executor runs quality gates using `tests/run.sh`\n2. **Phase A (New-Code):** Run tests from `new_tests.manifest` (A1 smoke → A2 unit → A3 integration)\n3. **Phase B (Regression):** Run full suite at specified levels (B1 smoke → B2 unit → B3 integration)\n4. On failure: hand report to Constructor, fix, re-run (up to 10 retries)\n5. Updates PROGRESS_BOARD.md: `smoke_passed` → `unit_tested` → `integration_tested` → `tested`\n6. Commits test logs and results\n\n**Outputs:**\n\n- `progress/sprint_${no}/sprint_${no}_tests.md`\n- `progress/sprint_${no}/test_run_*.log` (timestamped logs per gate)\n\n### Phase 5: Wrap-up (Documentation)\n\n1. Documentor Agent validates all Sprint documentation\n2. Verifies code snippets are copy-paste-able (no `exit` commands)\n3. Creates symbolic links in `progress/backlog/` for traceability\n4. Updates `README.md` with recent developments\n5. Commits documentation updates\n\n**Outputs:**\n\n- Updated `README.md`\n- Symbolic links in `progress/backlog/${BACKLOG_ITEM_ID}/`\n\n## Chapter editing rules\n\n**PROHIBITED:**\n\n- Do not modify the Implementation Plan in `PLAN.md`\n- Do not modify `Test data`\n- Do not modify status tokens in phase documents (owned by Product Owner)\n\n**ALLOWED EDITS:**\n\n- Design documents (`sprint_${no}_design.md`)\n- Implementation Notes (`sprint_${no}_implementation.md`) - excluding status tokens\n- Test documents (`sprint_${no}_tests.md`)\n- Analysis documents (`sprint_${no}_analysis.md`)\n- Proposed changes (`sprint_${no}_proposedchanges.md`)\n- More information needed (`sprint_${no}_openquestions.md`)\n- **EXCEPTION:** `PROGRESS_BOARD.md` - Agents update during their respective phases\n- **EXCEPTION:** `PLAN.md` - you can update status label from `Progress` to `Done` or `Failed`.\n\n**PREREQUISITES:**\n\n- Before implementation, verify the Product Owner has accepted the design (Status: `Accepted`)\n- Before editing any phase document, ensure you are in the correct phase\n\n## Implementation Sprints life-cycle\n\nImplementation Sprints are listed in `PLAN.md` with indicated status. The `Sprint` status follows a below state machine. Note that this FSM is fully owned by the Product Owner.\n\n```mermaid\nstateDiagram-v2\n    [*] --> Planned\n    Planned --> Progress\n    Progress --> Designed\n    Designed --> Implemented\n    Implemented --> Tested\n    Tested --> Done\n    \n    Progress --> Failed\n    Designed --> Failed\n    Implemented --> Failed\n\n    Planned --> Rejected\n    Progress --> Rejected\n    Designed --> Rejected\n    Implemented --> Rejected\n    Tested --> Rejected\n    \n    Planned --> Postponed\n    Progress --> Postponed\n    Designed --> Postponed\n    Implemented --> Postponed\n    Tested --> Postponed\n```\n\nEach specific Sprint's `design` follows:\n\n```mermaid\nstateDiagram-v2\n    [*] --> Proposed       # Set by Implementor\n    Proposed --> Accepted\n    Proposed --> Rejected\n    Accepted --> Done\n```\n\nAny implementor provided feedback in `Proposed changes` and `More information needed` chapters follows:\n\n```mermaid\nstateDiagram-v2\n    [*] --> Proposed        # Set by Implementor\n    Proposed --> Accepted\n    Proposed --> Postponed\n    Proposed --> Rejected\n```\n\n## Design\n\nDesign is owned by the Implementor and is stored in `progress/sprint_<id>/sprint_<id>_design.md` file for each Sprint.\n\nProduct Owner exceptionally may insert slight changes. Product owner owns `Status` line under phase chapter inserting here design state according to design's state machine defined in this document. Implementor NEVER touches the status, but his actions are driven by the status.\n\nGeneral template for the file:\n\n```markdown\n# Sprint <id> - design\n\n## <Backlog Item A>\n\nStatus: Progress\n\nDesign details for <Backlog Item A>\n\n## <Backlog Item B>\n\nStatus: Progress\n\nDesign details for <Backlog Item B>\n```\n\n## Implementation notes\n\nImplementations notes is owned by the Implementor and is stored in `progress/sprint_<id>/sprint_<id>_implementation.md` file for each Sprint.\n\nProduct Owner exceptionally may insert slight changes. Product owner owns `Status` line under phase chapter inserting here design state according to design's state machine defined in this document. Implementor NEVER touches the status, but his actions are driven by the status.\n\nGeneral template for the file:\n\n```markdown\n# Sprint <id> - Implementation Notes\n\n## <Backlog Item A>\n\nStatus: Progress\n\nDesign details for <Backlog Item A>\n\n## <Backlog Item B>\n\nStatus: Progress\n\nDesign details for <Backlog Item B>\n```\n\n## Management Skills\n\nThe following skills are available for artifact management outside the RUP cycle:\n\n| Skill | Purpose | Operations |\n|-------|---------|------------|\n| `/backlog` | Manage backlog items | `add`, `list`, `prioritize` |\n| `/sprint` | Manage sprints | `create`, `status`, `start`, `close` |\n| `/bug` | Handle bugs during sprints | `report`, `triage`, `list` |\n\nThese skills enforce the format constraints defined in the corresponding rule files.\n\n## Testing\n\n### Centralized Test Structure\n\nAll executable tests live in `tests/` at the repo root:\n\n```\ntests/\n├── smoke/           # Quick critical checks\n├── unit/            # Function-level tests\n├── integration/     # End-to-end tests\n├── manifests/       # Component test registries\n│   └── component_*.manifest\n└── run.sh           # Test runner entry point\n```\n\n**Principles:**\n\n- One file per component/domain, NOT per sprint\n- New test cases are appended to existing files\n- `run.sh` is the single entry point (`--smoke`, `--unit`, `--integration`)\n\n### General Testing Requirements\n\n1. Correlation must be tested with parallel executions proving that parallel clients will always have access to workflows triggered by them.\n\n2. Negative test may try to break the link between client call and the actual workflow run\n\nMore specific testing may be specified for each `Backlog Item` and `Sprint`.\n\n### Test Documentation Requirements\n\nAll test documentation in `progress/sprint_${no}/sprint_${no}_tests.md` MUST comply with:\n\n**Critical Requirements:**\n\n- ✅ All tests MUST be copy-paste-able shell sequences\n- ✅ Tests MUST be executable without modification (except tokens/secrets)\n- ✅ Expected output MUST be documented\n- ✅ Both success and error cases MUST be tested\n- ❌ **NEVER use `exit 1` or any exit command in copy/paste examples** (user terminal will close)\n- ✅ Prerequisites clearly listed\n- ✅ Verification steps provided\n- ✅ Test status recorded (PASS/FAIL)\n- ✅ Test summary table with results\n\n**Example Structure:**\n\n```markdown\n### Test 1: [Test Name]\n\n**Purpose:** [What this test validates]\n\n**Expected Outcome:** [What should happen]\n\n**Test Sequence:**\n```bash\n# Step 1: [description]\ncommand-to-execute\n\n# Step 2: [description]\nanother-command\n\n# Expected output:\n# [show expected output]\n\n# Verification:\n[how to verify success]\n```\n\n**Status:** [PASS | FAIL | PENDING]\n```\n\n### Implementation Documentation Requirements\n\nAll implementation documentation in `progress/sprint_${no}/sprint_${no}_implementation.md` MUST include:\n\n**User Documentation Section:**\n\n- Overview of what was implemented\n- Prerequisites clearly listed\n- Usage examples (copy-paste-able)\n- ❌ **No `exit` commands in examples**\n- Expected outputs shown\n- Error handling examples\n- Options/parameters documented\n- Special notes for edge cases\n\n**Example Structure:**\n\n```markdown\n#### Usage\n\n**Basic Usage:**\n```bash\n# Execute the script\n./script.sh [options]\n```\n\n**Examples:**\n\nExample 1: Normal operation\n```bash\n# Complete copy-paste example\n./script.sh --option value\n```\n\nExpected output:\n```\n[show expected output]\n```\n```\n\n## Backlog Traceability\n\nTo maintain complete requirement traceability from backlog items to implementation, symbolic links are created in the `progress/backlog/` directory structure.\n\n### Purpose\n\n- Complete traceability from requirement to implementation\n- Easy navigation: `progress/backlog/GH-15/` shows all GH-15 related documents\n- Cross-sprint visibility for multi-sprint backlog items\n- Historical audit trail\n\n### Structure\n\nFor each Backlog Item processed in a Sprint, create:\n\n```text\nprogress/backlog/${BACKLOG_ITEM_ID}/\n  ├── sprint_${no}_analysis.md -> ../../sprint_${no}/sprint_${no}_analysis.md\n  ├── sprint_${no}_design.md -> ../../sprint_${no}/sprint_${no}_design.md\n  ├── sprint_${no}_implementation.md -> ../../sprint_${no}/sprint_${no}_implementation.md\n  ├── sprint_${no}_tests.md -> ../../sprint_${no}/sprint_${no}_tests.md\n  └── sprint_${no}_documentation.md -> ../../sprint_${no}/sprint_${no}_documentation.md\n```\n\n### Creation Process\n\n**Step 1:** Identify Backlog Items from `PLAN.md` for current sprint\n\n**Step 2:** Create Backlog Item directory (if doesn't exist)\n\n```bash\nmkdir -p progress/backlog/[BACKLOG-ITEM-ID]\n```\n\n**Step 3:** Create symbolic links to all sprint documents\n\n```bash\ncd progress/backlog/[BACKLOG-ITEM-ID]\nln -sf ../../sprint_${no}/sprint_${no}_analysis.md .\nln -sf ../../sprint_${no}/sprint_${no}_design.md .\nln -sf ../../sprint_${no}/sprint_${no}_implementation.md .\nln -sf ../../sprint_${no}/sprint_${no}_tests.md .\nln -sf ../../sprint_${no}/sprint_${no}_documentation.md .\n```\n\n**Step 4:** Verify links work\n\n```bash\nls -la progress/backlog/[BACKLOG-ITEM-ID]/\n# Verify all links point to existing files\n```\n\n### Example\n\nSprint 15 with GH-14, GH-15, GH-16:\n\n```bash\nmkdir -p progress/backlog/{GH-14,GH-15,GH-16}\ncd progress/backlog/GH-14 && ln -sf ../../sprint_15/*.md . && cd ../..\ncd progress/backlog/GH-15 && ln -sf ../../sprint_15/*.md . && cd ../..\ncd progress/backlog/GH-16 && ln -sf ../../sprint_15/*.md . && cd ../..\n```\n\n### Responsibility\n\nThe **Documentor Agent** (Phase 5) is responsible for creating and maintaining these symbolic links during the Documentation phase.\n\n## Sprint Document Naming Conventions\n\nAll Sprint-related documents follow standardized naming:\n\n**Phase 1: Contracting**\n\n- `progress/sprint_${no}/sprint_${no}_contract_review_${cnt}.md`\n  - `${no}`: Sprint number\n  - `${cnt}`: Review iteration number (for multiple contracting sessions)\n\n**Phase 2: Inception (Analysis)**\n\n- `progress/sprint_${no}/sprint_${no}_analysis.md`\n- `progress/sprint_${no}/sprint_${no}_inception_review.md`\n\n**Phase 3: Elaboration (Design)**\n\n- `progress/sprint_${no}/sprint_${no}_design.md`\n- `progress/sprint_${no}/sprint_${no}_elaboration_review_${cnt}.md`\n  - `${cnt}`: Review iteration number (for design revisions)\n\n**Phase 4: Construction (Implementation)**\n\n- `progress/sprint_${no}/sprint_${no}_implementation.md`\n- `progress/sprint_${no}/sprint_${no}_tests.md`\n- Code artifacts (varies by project)\n\n**Phase 5: Documentation**\n\n- Document rules\n\n### Editing\n\n1. Use Markdown\n\n2. Do not use any indention under chapters. Each paragraph starts always at column zero. Exception are enumerations.\n\n3. Always add an empty line before any code blocks, enumerations. Follow Markdown linting rules.\n\n4. Always add an empty line after chapters, list headers. Follow Markdown linting rules.\n\n### Content ownership\n\n**Document Ownership by Agent:**\n\n| Document | Owner | Editing Rules |\n|----------|-------|---------------|\n| `PLAN.md` | Product Owner | Read-only for agents |\n| `BACKLOG.md` | Product Owner | Read-only for agents |\n| `PROGRESS_BOARD.md` | All Agents | Update during respective phases |\n| Analysis documents | Analyst Agent | Create and own during Inception |\n| Design documents | Designer Agent | Create and own during Elaboration |\n| Implementation docs | Constructor Agent | Create and own during Construction |\n| Test documents | Constructor Agent | Create and own during Construction |\n| Documentation summary | Documentor Agent | Create and own during Documentation |\n| Proposed changes | Any Agent | Append-only, never edit existing |\n| Open questions | Any Agent | Append-only, never edit existing |\n\n**Key Rules:**\n\n1. Any change to completed work or Implementation Plan (`PLAN.md`) goes through `Proposed changes` and `More information needed` process.\n\n2. Never edit documents from other Sprints - only current Sprint documents.\n\n3. Never edit status tokens - these are owned by Product Owner.\n\n4. Design Status lines in phase documents are owned by Product Owner except initial \"Proposed\" status set by Designer Agent.\n\n5.
