Existing administrator credentials must not become CI credentials.\n\nProposed E.1 entry points, to implement under its accepted test design:\n\n```bash\nbash scripts/cicd-build.sh\nbash scripts/cicd-integration-test.sh .local-apex/releases/lab.tar\nbash scripts/cicd-promote.sh .local-apex/releases/lab.tar user_acceptance_test\nbash tests/run.sh --smoke\nbash tests/run.sh --unit\nbash tests/run.sh --integration\n```\n\nBuild must validate the complete source set and package a clean reviewed revision as `.local-apex/releases/lab.tar`, with its commit/tool manifest and external SHA-256 receipt. Reject a dirty product source set or files missing from the declared complete-release/assembly-stage contract. A seed/Foundation-only artifact explicitly contains no application export; its component-specific assembly checks must not be reported as passing the full greeting release gates. Packaging does not export live DEV during promotion. Do not overwrite a retained archive: subsequent archives get distinct retained identities/paths. The example path identifies the selected run's archive. integration_test takes that archive, records its SHA-256/accepted commit, declared starting baseline and controlled nonsecret inputs, and proves clean complete installation plus the one declared additive upgrade path in disposable targets. Assembly archives instead qualify against their declared stage and component-specific checks; they cannot claim complete-release integration_test. Promotion rejects missing integration_test, changed bytes, an unsupported target start or mismatched configuration evidence. It uses those same qualified bytes; PR candidate validation is a separate gate. The project controller must run SQL from the verified archive rather than mutable checkout files. These entry points are proposed, not scripts delivered in I.1.\n\nSmoke verifies target identity, database/APEX version, expected application identity, installed component versions and an HTTP route; declared initial assembly explicitly checks absence where appropriate and records unavailable full-application cases as not applicable, never passed; incorrect targets or incompatible/unknown version combinations fail before deployment writes. Unit tests use small PL/SQL assertions for composition/validation against dedicated test fixtures; no additional test framework is proposed. Integration qualifies/deploys and repeat-deploys the same archive, retaining seeded history. A retry may reuse it only after safe repeat/resume is established. A forward repair containing changed code/migrations uses a new reviewed archive qualified against the approved partial starting state; preserve the original archive and receipts. Every entry point returns zero only when all assertions pass, prints case IDs and outcomes, and retains a redacted timestamped log. The runner must support the method's `--new-only` manifest filter; unfiltered invocation is regression.\n\n## Underlying tool commands\n\nThese interfaces are also **proposed and unrun**. Their referenced SQL files are to be created in E.1, not treated as existing working examples.\n\n```bash\nsql -version\njava -version\nsql -name lab-dev @deploy/export.sql\nsql -name integration_test @deploy/validate.sql\nsql -name integration_test @deploy/install.sql\nsql -name user_acceptance_test @deploy/validate.sql\nsql -name user_acceptance_test @deploy/install.sql\nsql -name user_acceptance_test @tests/unit/greeting.sql\n```\n\n`integration_test` is a protected mapping to the disposable integration_test role; candidate jobs use its separately reset candidate role, never user_acceptance_test. The integration_test/promotion controllers establish the verified archive execution directory and input mapping before invoking installation SQL. Low-level SQL commands alone are not a replacement for artifact/ownership/integration_test gates.\n\n`export.sql` loads `APP_ID` from the verified DEV mapping and runs:\n\n```text\napex export -applicationid &APP_ID -split -skipExportDate -expOriginalIds -expSupportingObjects N\n```\n\n`validate.sql` guards identity, expected target revision, live fingerprints, state, component compatibility and the upgrade path before the first command below. The deployment controller repeats these checks under exclusive ownership before installation; validation alone does not reserve the target. `install.sql` guards identity, runs the second, sets the explicit APEX installation context, imports the split metadata and applies nonsecret target settings:\n\n```text\nlb validate -changelog-file db/changelog.xml\nlb update -changelog-file db/changelog.xml\n```\n\nOptions derive from the [APEX 24.2 export reference](https://docs.oracle.com/en/database/oracle/apex/24.2/aeadm/exporting-one-or-more-applications.html) and [SQLcl 25.3 Liquibase reference](https://docs.oracle.com/en/database/oracle/sql-developer-command-line/25.3/sqcug/liquibase.html). Prove shell exit behavior for SQL, Liquibase and APEX failures with negative cases; merely printing an error is insufficient.\n\n## Required evidence, not tests implemented in I.1\n\n**SM-1:** Expected target/version/application identity and HTTP route succeed; wrong schema/workspace/application mapping is rejected without writes.\n\n**SM-2:** A reviewed Foundation/Hello/World version triple and supported upgrade path are accepted. An incompatible Hello/World pair, unsupported Foundation version, unexpectedly missing metadata and a mismatch between APEX labels and registry are rejected before deployment writes. Preserve the observed installed set on rejection. Do not infer compatibility from matching major versions alone.\n\n**UT-1:** Seed composition is exactly `Hello World`; prefixes, Unicode and 100-character boundary entries work; empty, oversized and inactive selections fail. **UT-2:** Default ownership/deactivation rules and atomic history insertion hold; configuration changes leave earlier snapshots unchanged.\n\n**IT-1:** Export twice without edits and inspect differences. Validate the current PR candidate in a disposable area; after merge build one accepted-revision archive, qualify those exact bytes for clean reconstruction and the declared additive upgrade, then promote them to user_acceptance_test. Record unchanged archive hash, accepted commit, integration_test/deployment receipts, target configuration identity and observed greeting. Reject stale PR evidence, changed bytes, missing integration_test and unsupported starting versions. Verify disposable reset cannot touch user_acceptance_test history. **IT-2:** Upgrade with an additive database change and a Hello or World change; all 20 prior sentinel history rows retain their complete values. Repeat the archive: migrations/seeds do not duplicate, user_acceptance_test settings remain correct, and DEV data is unchanged. **IT-3:** A failing precheck blocks deployment; a separate failure after committed additive DDL stops subsequent steps. Inspect live partial definitions/version records and authorize a recovery linked to the failed attempt and current generation. Inject an intervening unexplained change and reject recovery until reviewed reconciliation. Qualify the selected new repair archive against the inspected partial state, recheck that starting state under ownership, repair and prove history preservation. Separately establish when unchanged bytes can safely resume. Incomplete observation/unsupported recovery stays blocked; logs alone do not prove what committed. A coordinated upgrade cannot report success with mixed versions. **IT-4:** From the reviewed greenfield seed and bootstrapped control authority, verify the explicit assembly manifest: Foundation-only component checks succeed and release A's reservation while root maintenance blocks ordinary/incomplete dependent behavior; deliberately absent Hello/World are recorded without fake versions. Allow only the declared subsequent assembly stages. Require the complete approved triple for the first operational user_acceptance_test release. The same missing metadata on a previously complete target is rejected. Show Foundation, Hello and World labels, compare them with artifact metadata and installed records, and verify accepted/rejected complete version sets. These are future cases, not tests implemented in I.1.\n\nAutomated browser checks sign in, edit each component independently, submit a greeting, reject invalid values, display escaped markup, refresh history and check pagination with 21 rows. SQL and HTTP 200 cannot substitute for that evidence. Manual walkthroughs supplement these automated checks. E.1 covers its accepted thin slice; full-page behavior remains a release gate if deferred from the prototype.\n\nAll fixtures are synthetic and confined to dedicated lab targets. Never discard retained history, touch unrelated resources or log secrets; scope teardown to the owned disposable experiment instance. Use local services for execution after tools are provisioned. Record prerequisite failures separately from product failures. Before activating E.1, obtain Product Owner acceptance of this command contract; before claiming tests pass, implement and actually execute them.\n\n## Collaboration evidence proposed after use-case refinement\n\n**IT-5 — Core multi-developer journey:** A starts from the reviewed greenfield seed (or the separately verified UC-12 adoption path), reserves and delivers Foundation through a task branch/PR, passes component-specific assembly checks and releases ownership while the root remains maintenance. Foundation must reconstruct without Hello/World-owned objects. No completed Hello/World application is supplied to bypass their authoring work. From its accepted baseline, B and C reserve and develop Hello and World on separate task branches/targets. The two different-component reservations must coexist. Capture their actual APEX definitions through SQLcl into the appropriate checkout; verify canonical identity/path handling, deletion reconciliation and an unchanged second capture. Reject wrong source mappings or unsafe overwrites of uncommitted work. Merge, import the combined SQL into the validation target and show both intended changes, correct component versions and retained history. Record which state was private/shared. B/C each add a component-owned page/navigation entry so both exports touch shared bookkeeping; retain both additions. After B's acceptance, C incorporates B's accepted revision without treating inherited Hello changes as new C-authored edits. Preserve provenance and revalidate the candidate. The complete accepted application deploys under target ownership without reacquiring authoring reservations for completed/unchanged components.\n\n**IT-6 — Scope enforcement and exceptional reconciliation:** Attempt to capture or integrate a change outside semantic ownership and reject it, including an unauthorized change to shared composition/security/controller behavior. Allow only bounded validated generated-file reconciliation. Discover additional task scope, preserve work, atomically request the complete expanded set and return a conflict if unavailable; verify no partial acquisition or indefinite hold-and-wait. Revalidate the base before resuming. In an explicitly controlled out-of-tool/break-glass variation, introduce same-page/shared version metadata conflicts, then require reviewed reconciliation and renewed import/behavior checks. This variation does not permit two managed tasks to own Hello concurrently. Exercise a page deletion and colliding/ordered database changes. Reject missing dependencies, duplicate page/component identities and stale exports rather than silently dropping work. Use native Working Copy repair only as a break-glass variation, returning the change through reconciliation/PR. Its replacement semantics do not prove Git merge behavior or schema isolation.\n\nE.1 must refine and validate a feasible task-area layout within the selected single-host proof and validate export identity stability before this experiment. These are future case specifications only. No task branches, developer schemas, APEX copies or executable tests were created in I.1.\n\nFor the PR flow, IT-5/6 must validate the combined task/target-branch candidate before merge. A changed PR or integration baseline invalidates an earlier result until the updated candidate passes. A failed foundation/compatibility or APEX integration gate blocks merge; CD uses the accepted merged revision. These are pipeline acceptance requirements, not a workflow implemented in I.1.\n\n## Drift, concurrency and state evidence proposed for E.1\n\n**SM-3:** A stale expected generation or changed live APEX fingerprint blocks installation without changing the running target to failed. Missing/mismatched fingerprint profiles or baseline metadata also block an existing-target deploy. Only explicitly approved first provisioning may start without an installed base.\n\n**IT-7 — Drift and break-glass reconciliation:** Establish a successful baseline; recompute its fingerprint without edits and obtain the same result. Change a page in App Builder without changing its version; detect drift and preserve the changed source before any deployment overwrite. Check page deletion/shared metadata coverage and a separate manual schema change. Reconcile a retained emergency fix through a reviewed branch and controlled deployment. Record exact export options and any target-configuration differences. Do not treat an old artifact checksum or migration ledger alone as the live-content check.\n\n**IT-8 — Concurrent deployment:** Two attempts expect the same target revision. Only the owner passing a fresh guarded check may mutate it; the other is rejected or rechecks and rejects the advanced generation. Cover the full application-import scope and dependency versions. Demonstrate how emergency editing is excluded from the check/write interval; our own mutex does not lock App Builder itself. Prove ownership persists across DDL commits, SQLcl subprocesses and connection failure; after restart inspect durable authority/attempt records before recovery. A transaction row lock released by a commit is insufficient.\n\n**IT-9 — State inheritance and recovery:** An untagged Hello page inherits Hello maintenance. An explicitly ready child cannot bypass a blocked ancestor; a failed Foundation blocks dependent actions. Exercise direct URLs, form submits and existing sessions, not merely hidden navigation. Prove maintenance → ready requires all checks, mutation failure remains blocked with a reason, and an interrupted controller cannot silently restore readiness. Reject invalid/missing root state and preserve target state across imports. Verify controlled diagnostics remain usable. After preliminary installation/integrity checks, a dedicated deployment-scoped verifier must exercise the real greeting/history path while ordinary direct submissions and existing sessions remain blocked. Bypass only the active deployment-maintenance restriction; failed/unknown dependencies and normal authorization still block, and negative state tests must observe them. Cover draining/fencing active mutations, no unrestricted import interval, stale verifier permission and interruption. Revoke permission and reconcile final availability/configuration/fingerprint evidence before publishing readiness. Identify verifier-created synthetic rows without changing retained history. Demonstrate authorized failed → maintenance → ready repair with history retained and consistent state/version/fingerprint receipts.\n\nAll cases above are specifications only. No SQL, test harness, drift collector, state registry or deployment lock has been implemented or exercised in I.1.\n\n\n## Initial adoption and complete developer journey\n\n**IT-10 — Bootstrap the golden source (UC-12):** Capture an identified source application and its required database/static dependencies without committing secrets or runtime business data. Record versions/profile/provenance, reconstruct the candidate Git revision in a controlled target and compare required definitions/behavior before accepting the baseline. Missing dependencies, an inconsistent capture, a populated unverified target or unsafe repository overwrite must block adoption. Prove that subsequent release packaging uses the accepted Git revision, not a fresh developer export.\n\n**IT-11 — Developer onboarding and complete cycle (UC-8/9/10/7/4/11):** Start with a documented workstation/access state, verify supported software and permissions, obtain a private development area on the selected single host, and associate it with a task branch/base. Edit a focused page, capture/review/commit, submit and revise a PR, validate/merge, deploy, verify behavior/version/history/receipts, then refresh safely for the next task. Record the environment variant actually tested; do not claim both variants passed from one run. Missing permissions/tooling and uncaptured work must produce actionable blockers rather than silent data loss.\n\nIT-10/11 are future evidence requirements, not implemented or executed tests. The existing smoke/unit/integration command contract remains proposed; Elaboration refines the bounded prototype cases before construction.\n\n\n**IT-12 — Core early same-component rejection:** After the A/B/C component delivery cycle, synchronize B and C to request Hello in UC-9 from the same accepted base. Exactly one atomic reservation succeeds; the other returns a conflict identifying the owner/task before creating its managed branch, provisioning its task area or permitting editing. Assert no code/version/runtime-state change from the rejected request. Repeat with the other developer winning. Show that reservations of Hello and World can coexist and that reserving Hello prevents a competing task even on another Hello page. After verified completion or safe cancellation, the loser must obtain fresh ownership against the new accepted baseline. Validate stale-token rejection after authorized reassignment and atomic all-or-none acquisition for a change spanning multiple components.\n\nIT-5 and IT-12 are required proof-of-concept evidence for the multi-developer design. IT-8 separately covers the target deployment race; it cannot substitute for early task rejection. These specifications have not been implemented or executed in I.1.\n\n## Automation interface validation\n\nSRS FR-13 is a foundation requirement. Validate architecture inventory AI-1–10 during Elaboration, at the depth of each approved experiment. For each entry record the exact tool/server versions, executable command or API sequence, target and required grants, inputs/outputs, successful unattended run, relevant rejection/failure case, exit status and redacted evidence. Mark each entry validated, failed or not yet exercised; a proposed API or successful administrator-only run does not establish routine CI readiness. This evidence requirement is part of selecting the tools, not a separate product feature.\n\nUse the established cases rather than duplicating them: SM-1/IT-11 for connection/provisioning; IT-1/5/10 for capture/import; IT-2/3 for migrations; SM-3/IT-7 for drift; IT-8/9/12 and SM-2 for release/state/version/ownership controls; UT-1/2 and automated browser assertions for application behavior; IT-5/11 and scoped lifecycle experiments for orchestration/infrastructure. Recovery after an injected failure must also execute through the supported command/API path after the required human decision. Record any uncovered operation as an architectural gap and refine the plan before claiming the automation requirement satisfied. No interface validation or browser test implementation is claimed in I.1.\n\n## Elaboration order and architecture exit\n\nRefine PBI-011 into bounded children at E.1 setup/design, with approval before construction. First prove control bootstrap, topology/permissions and minimal atomic reservation/reassignment with concurrent clients; ownership feasibility must not wait until the full system exists. Then prove initial assembly, capture/identity and combined reconstruction, immutable archive integration_test, guarded delivery/verification/repair, and the complete A/B/C cycle including the Hello race. Reuse the cases above rather than creating a feature per mechanism.\n\nLifecycle Architecture requires evidence for these core controls under the selected topology and supported clean/additive-upgrade/repair paths. Complete page layouts, exhaustive input combinations and additional workstation variants may remain for Construction. If critical feasibility remains open, propose further Elaboration instead of declaring the architecture stable. This is an experiment/exit recommendation, not an E.1 activation or child-PBI assignment.\n\n### Bounded initial-release subcase\n\nRun `bash tests/run.sh --integration --new-only tests/manifests/initial_release.manifest` after initial Q/user_acceptance_test publication and explicit post-delivery sentinel preparation. This read-only delivery repeat check compares all retained history/configuration fields, requires matching successful archive receipts and live APEX fingerprints, and checks the fixture remains repeatable. It verifies the existing initial result; it does not perform fresh reconstruction. Full IT-1 upgrade/repeat-import and IT-2 patch criteria remain pending. Browser verification is separate, as documented in the E.1 test evidence.\n\n### Selected Hello patch and protection increment\n\nRun `bash tests/run.sh --integration --new-only tests/manifests/patch_protections.manifest` for IT-2, SM-2 and the focused IT-7 page-drift slice after both patch integration_tests and user_acceptance_test publication. IT-2 verifies identical-byte receipts for fresh/initial-upgrade starts, live fingerprints and field-level preservation of the retained 21-row baselines; its correction fixture rolls back. SM-2 exercises the incompatible proposal gate with before/after target/control comparisons. IT-7 checks real Oracle error propagation versus stored diagnostic text, injects the separately authored native page fixture, rejects drift and explicitly restores the original fingerprint while preserving data.\n\nThe fixture sequence replaces user_acceptance_test APEX definitions and may require runtime re-login; stop other user_acceptance_test authoring/deployment first. It does not rebuild/reset retained user_acceptance_test or install business seed. The initial-release manifest applies only while both live targets still contain that initial release; it is historical after upgrading. The full IT-1 candidate/reconstruction gate and broader concurrency, schema drift, maintenance/verifier fencing and repair acceptance remain open; passing the selected manifest is not a full integration/sprint-completion claim.\n\n### Fresh walkthrough lifecycle\n\nRun `bash tests/run.sh --unit --new-only tests/manifests/walkthrough_lifecycle.manifest` for the cleanup ownership boundary: allow only the known lab inventory; reject unrelated consumers of named volumes/network/secret and unexpected mounts/images before mutation. These local policy tests do not substitute for Oracle isolation or concurrency tests. After A–H, run `bash tests/run.sh --integration --new-only tests/manifests/walkthrough_lifecycle.manifest` for the registered IT-2 result/preservation assertions against this fresh cycle; it does not rerun cleanup.\n\nThe same focused unit manifest also tests infrastructure exclusion, control-only setup, single-area provisioning/reset and developer password/profile scope. These mocks test responsibility boundaries, not Oracle concurrency.\n\nThe destructive fresh-start experiment is a separate explicit operator sequence in [deploy.md](deploy.md#fresh-walkthrough-infrastructure-sequence): prerequisites, named host preparation, complete lab cleanup and fresh database/APEX/ORDS installation. It must first prove **zero project schemas/workspaces immediately after infrastructure installation**. Then explicitly initialize project control, provision each named area, configure each developer with only their assigned password, and run readiness/isolation checks before A–H. Provisioning and private developer setup must not be hidden in infrastructure installation. It is not part of ordinary regression and must never be invoked as a hidden fixture reset. The A–H rehearsal then executes reviewed source installation, new integration_test/promotion receipts, both patch starts, this-cycle field preservation and selected rejections/restoration. CLI runtime examples are explicitly synthetic verification; browser checkpoints remain separate in live mode. Archives/credentials remain on the host; no prior business history is restored. Broader concurrency/maintenance/recovery criteria remain unchanged.\n\n## C browser-verification retry control\n\nRun `bash tests/run.sh --unit --new-only tests/manifests/development_verification.manifest` for the selected C checkpoint loop: retry a missing required snapshot, exit on q, and stop on unexpected SQL failure. These control-flow tests stub Oracle/browser boundaries. The exact saved Hello World by VERIFIER remains required; other text may be valid business data but does not satisfy that walkthrough fixture. See [C verification steps](../progress/E.1/cicd-walkthrough.md#c-deliver-world-and-complete-the-assembly) and [observed results](../progress/E.1/sprint_E.1_tests.md).\n\n## Release browser-verification retry and bounded UAT resume\n\nThe same verification manifest now also checks D/E/F's missing-fixture retry, q and unexpected SQL failure paths. E alone adds a restricted initial 1.0.0 continuation after ORA-20619: validate the same archive/epoch/owner and root/native checkpoint, rerun archived integrity and rollback unit checks, repeat the browser assertion and then use archived publication. No migration/import/reset or new attempt is allowed. See [E continuation](../progress/E.1/cicd-walkthrough.md#resume-e-at-its-restricted-verification-checkpoint) and [operator guidance](../progress/E.1/user_manual.md#resume-initial-uat-verification).\n\n## Post-publication upgrade-example preparation\n\nE's announced UAT history fixture must work when active Hello/World entries were explicitly selected in the UI and a default remains unset. It selects active entries explicitly, preserves configuration and old history, and appends only the missing snapshots to reach twenty. A bounded post-publication continuation requires the same ready initial archive/live fingerprint and compares an existing preservation baseline rather than replacing it. See [E continuation](../progress/E.1/cicd-walkthrough.md#resume-e-after-publication-prepare-upgrade-examples) and [operator guidance](../progress/E.1/user_manual.md#resume-uat-upgrade-example-preparation). This is test-data preparation, not failed-deployment repair.\n\n## Manual Page Designer drift demonstration\n\n[H.1](../progress/E.1/cicd-walkthrough.md#h1-change-a-page-in-app-builder) adds an operator-executed variation of the selected IT-7/PBI-011.7 page-drift check, with evidence under PBI-012 and UC-5/FR-10. Start from a matching published patch; save a single reversible page-title edit in E1_UAT without changing versions; require the actual preflight's specific drift rejection and unchanged snapshots. Restore the exact property and require the original published fingerprint. Temporary workspace developer access is removed after the demonstration. [Operator guidance](../progress/E.1/user_manual.md#manual-page-designer-drift-demonstration) links the same procedure. This variation is documented for manual execution; no new browser execution is claimed. Schema drift, general reconciliation and concurrency remain outside this demonstration.\n\n## Drift check outcomes\n\nThe [three demonstrations](../progress/E.1/cicd-walkthrough.md#h-reject-deployment-over-unexpected-live-change) use the same operator drift command on unchanged content, a manual Page Designer edit and a scripted fixture edit. The checker classifies only a specific successful preflight as NO DRIFT and only a specific nonzero fingerprint rejection as DETECTED DRIFT; arbitrary errors cannot pass. Both recognized outcomes require unchanged before/after target/control snapshots. Default checker success means the inspection/protection assertion passed; it does not authorize deployment over drift. Optional --expect-clean/--expect-drift asserts an expected phase for automated testing. H requires clean before injection/restoration completion and drift after injection. [The operator manual](../progress/E.1/user_manual.md#one-drift-check-three-demonstrations) describes these same outcomes.\n\nRun `bash tests/run.sh --unit --new-only tests/manifests/protection_outcomes.manifest` for clean, drift, unexpected Oracle failure, silent failure, missing expected drift, unexpected baseline drift and compatibility regression cases. These focused tests classify captured controller output; they do not perform browser editing or deployment.\n\n## Read-only drift source review\n\nThe [H.1 review/decision/restoration steps](../progress/E.1/cicd-walkthrough.md#h1-change-a-page-in-app-builder) and [operator explanation](../progress/E.1/user_manual.md#inspect-manual-drift-before-deciding-restoration) separate source comparison from the user's decision to restore edited properties. cicd-drift-diff.py requires the exact successful patch/source/configuration receipt, captures native APEX source and checks stable fingerprint/receipt across capture. It prints page attribute differences and preserves both source trees, review.diff and raw.diff. Only target offset lines and generated Manifest comments are removed in the shorter comparison; behavior, unknown differences and deployment metadata remain visible. No source import, data mutation, Git update or baseline adoption occurs.\n\nRun `bash tests/run.sh --unit --new-only tests/manifests/drift_review.manifest` for five underlying cases: bounded normalization with complete raw diff, added/deleted source, escaped region names, unrelated receipt rejection and unchanged source. The native manual-drift check reports the current Home title/region changes without restoration. This is APEX source review, not a general SQL semantic diff or failed-deployment recovery tool. Restoration acceptance still requires the original published live fingerprint, not merely a clean text diff.\n\nThe operator entry point for that source review is `bash scripts/cicd-protection-check.sh drift --show`. After a recognized drift rejection and unchanged-snapshot checks, it invokes the read-only comparison helper; a clean result reports no unexpected differences to display. Review failures propagate as nonzero without restoration. H.2 uses --expect-drift --show together. The protection-outcome manifest also covers --show with expected outcomes, rejection of compatibility --show and conflicting/duplicate flags.\n\n\n## Review repair and clean-checkout validation\n\n`bash scripts/cicd-walkthrough-I_repair.sh --rehearse` explicitly executes the selected native BUG-7 failure/observation/repair/negative/repeat experiment in disposable integration. It requires a fresh case, known ready 1.0.1 start and at least twenty baseline rows. It does not replace full IT-3/9 concurrency/unknown-state coverage or browser validation. [Recorded proof](../progress/E.1/tests/recovery_proof_20261018.txt) shows 21 preserved rows/settings and retained failure/success history. [Independent checkout procedure](deploy.md#independent-checkout-rehearsal-while-retaining-the-current-lab) validates clean source/inputs in a separate VM while preserving the retained lab intact; its results must be recorded before claiming that gate passed.\n\n## E.3 implemented pipeline checks\n\nThe [E.3 specification](../progress/E.3/sprint_E.3_design.md#test-specification)\nretains SM-1/IT-1/IT-2. These parent commands perform fixed **reference-run\nregression**, not selected-LAB replay acceptance:\n\n```bash\nbash tests/run.sh --smoke --new-only progress/E.3/new_tests.manifest\nbash tests/run.sh --integration --new-only progress/E.3/new_tests.manifest\nbash tests/run.sh --smoke\n```\n\nThe recorded historical totals are 1/1, 2/2 and 2/2. SM-1 statically checks the\nexample workflows, configuration boundary and nine PR classification fixtures.\nIT-1/IT-2 hard-code rstyczynski/APEX_CICD_E3 and\nprogress/E.3/tests/github-environment-journey.json: deployment 41679427616,\nqualification 41679432928, tag v1.0.4. They read existing public run metadata and\ndownload retained artifacts into disposable directories; they do not dispatch\nworkflows, create a clone with a missing file or execute native verification.\nA new download is not a new workflow result, and artifacts can expire.\n\nIT-1 checks the recorded PR/tag/archive/changelog identities and reported setup,\npreservation and cleanup steps. IT-2 checks the recorded qualification receipt,\npositive log and the specific missing-file ERROR/count/path using the shared\nassertion. Compatibility with its path-less legacy receipt is allowed only for\nthe pinned original source/run/path; new-format receipts require the selected\npath and controller identity. Historical receipts are never rewritten.\n\nPlatform smoke makes a current HTTP probe using the **seed** environment's ORDS\nand application variables. It requires permission to read that seed environment;\npublic source access or LAB ownership is insufficient. It checks a login form,\nnot LAB_USER authentication or the selected LAB's current state. Parent checks\nneed gh, Git, jq, Node, curl, shasum and awk; SM-1 also needs actionlint. They\nneed no local database passwords or private snapshots.\n\nFor a new LAB replay, use the operator repository and actual source/release PRs,\ngenerated tag/source SHA, deployment receipt/archive identity and native logs,\nseparate qualification run if performed, and browser title/history observations.\nPassing seed readers cannot substitute for those checks or be required of every\nLAB operator. Wider drift/fencing/identity and new-host reproduction remain deferred.\n\nThe review's focused offline classifier/receipt regression is separate:\n\n```bash\nbash tests/unit/test_missing_changeset.sh\n```\n\nIt exercises the actual assertions against retained and synthetic diagnostic\nfixtures, including unrelated errors, wrong paths, incomplete output, ANSI and\nwrapped paths, and new/legacy receipt constraints. It does not connect to Oracle,\ndispatch qualification or establish a new native runtime success.\n\n\n## E.4 qualified experiment boundary\n\nThe [accepted E.4 design](../progress/E.4/sprint_E.4_design.md) and\n[current evidence](../progress/E.4/sprint_E.4_tests.md) qualify three independent\nnative author processes, a later announced release branch, separate A/C opt-in and\nmanager admission, B preservation, and supported central DEV synchronization/repeat.\nOne Codex operator simulated the roles; no three-human study or production fencing\nis claimed. The native checksum changed after exact title restoration because the\nonly remaining full-source difference was p_version_scn; the Product Owner approved\nreviewed counter-only recovery. Status API operations also change fingerprints.\nThe [manual](../progress/E.4/user_manual.md) owns the bounded operating procedure;\n[handover](../progress/E.4/sprint_E.4_handover.md) owns review/acceptance. Each must pass before the next:\\n\\n| Gate | Command | Condition |\\n|------|---------|-----------|\\n| A1 Smoke | `tests/run.sh --smoke --new-only progress/sprint_N/new_tests.manifest` | if `Test:` includes `smoke` |\\n| A2 Unit | `tests/run.sh --unit --new-only progress/sprint_N/new_tests.manifest` | if `Test:` includes `unit` |\\n| A3 Integration | `tests/run.sh --integration --new-only progress/sprint_N/new_tests.manifest` | if `Test:` includes `integration` |\\n\\nA1 fail = skip A2/A3 (build is too broken).\\n\\n### Phase B: Regression Gates (per `Regression:` parameter)\\n\\nRun after Phase A passes. Full suite unless `Regression scope:` is set:\\n\\n| Gate | Command | Condition |\\n|------|---------|-----------|\\n| B1 Smoke | `tests/run.sh --smoke [--component <scope>]` | if `Regression:` includes `smoke` |\\n| B2 Unit | `tests/run.sh --unit [--component <scope>]` | if `Regression:` includes `unit` |\\n| B3 Integration | `tests/run.sh --integration [--component <scope>]` | if `Regression:` includes `integration` |\\n\\n### Retry Policy\\n\\n| Mode | Retries 1-4 | Retry 5 | Retries 6-10 | After 10 |\\n|------|-------------|---------|--------------|----------|\\n| Managed | Auto fix-and-rerun | Human escalation | Continue if approved | Sprint `failed` |\\n| YOLO | Auto fix-and-rerun | Auto | Auto | Sprint `failed` |\\n\\nSee `rules/generic/test_failure_classification.md` for flaky vs broken handling.\\n\\n### YOLO Mode Thresholds\\n\\n| Test Level | Required Pass Rate |\\n|------------|-------------------|\\n| Smoke | 100% (no exceptions) |\\n| Unit | 100% (no exceptions) |\\n| Integration | ≥80% with failures documented |\\n\\n### On Failure\\n\\n1. Hand failure report to Constructor (Phase 3)\\n2. Constructor fixes the code\\n3. Test Executor re-runs the failing gate\\n4. Repeat until pass or retries exhausted\\n\\nProduce `progress/sprint_N/sprint_N_tests.md` with `## Artifacts` listing all log paths.\\n\\nUpdate `PROGRESS_BOARD.md`: Items → `smoke_passed` / `unit_tested` / `integration_tested` / `tested` (or `failed`); Sprint → `implemented` / `implemented_partially` / `failed`.\\n\\n**Commit:** `test(sprint-N): quality gates — [pass/fail summary]` · Push.\\n\\n**Phase 5 only after all gates pass (or YOLO threshold met). If retries exhausted → mark `failed`, still run Phase 5 for documentation.**\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Bug Handling\\n\\nBugs discovered during phases follow `rules/generic/bug_policy.md`:\\n\\n- **Fold-in:** Fix as part of current backlog item (default)\\n- **Promote:** Create new backlog item if scope expands\\n\\nRegister bugs in `progress/sprint_N/sprint_N_bugs.md`.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Phase 5: Execute Wrap-up\\n\\nRead `.claude/commands/agents/agent-documentor.md` and execute with these focuses:\\n\\n1. **Update `README.md`** — Add `### Sprint N — [title]` to Recent Updates section\\n2. **Create backlog traceability symlinks** in `progress/backlog/[ITEM-ID]/`\\n3. **Inline compliance check** (verify before committing):\\n   - All sprint artifacts exist: `setup.md`, `design.md`, `implementation.md`, `tests.md`, log files\\n   - No `exit` commands in copy-paste blocks\\n   - All log file paths listed in `tests.md ## Artifacts`\\n4. **Verify PROGRESS_BOARD.md** final state is correct\\n\\n**Commit:** `docs(sprint-N): update README and backlog traceability` · Push.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Step 6: Final Summary (MANDATORY)\\n\\n**CRITICAL**: After all phases complete, you MUST provide this summary.\\n\\n```markdown\\n# RUP Cycle — Sprint [N] Completion Report\\n\\nSprint: N | Mode: [YOLO|managed] | Status: [implemented|implemented_partially|failed]\\n\\n## Phases Executed\\n\\n| Phase | Status | Output |\\n|-------|--------|--------|\\n| Phase 1 Setup | done | sprint_N_setup.md |\\n| Phase 2 Design | done | sprint_N_design.md (includes test spec) |\\n| Phase 3 Construction | done | sprint_N_implementation.md |\\n| Phase 4 Quality Gates | [pass/fail] | [N] log files + sprint_N_tests.md |\\n| Phase 5 Wrap-up | done | README + backlog traceability |\\n\\n## Backlog Items\\n\\n| Item | Status | Tests |\\n|------|--------|-------|\\n| PBI-N | tested/failed | N pass / N fail |\\n\\n## Quality Gates\\n\\n| Gate | Result | Retries |\\n|------|--------|---------|\\n| A1 Smoke | [pass/skip/fail] | N |\\n| A2 Unit | [pass/skip/fail] | N |\\n| A3 Integration | [pass/skip/fail] | N |\\n| B1 Smoke | [pass/skip/fail] | N |\\n| B2 Unit | [pass/skip/fail] | N |\\n| B3 Integration | [pass/skip/fail] | N |\\n\\n## Test Parameters\\n\\n- Test: [value]\\n- Regression: [value]\\n- Regression scope: [component or \\\"full suite\\\"]\\n- Flaky tests deferred: [list or \\\"None\\\"]\\n\\n## Commits\\n\\n- [hash] docs(sprint-N): setup phase — contract and analysis\\n- [hash] docs(sprint-N): design, test spec, and test skeletons\\n- [hash] feat(sprint-N): implement [description]\\n- [hash] test(sprint-N): quality gates — [summary]\\n- [hash] docs(sprint-N): update README and backlog traceability\\n\\n## Files Modified\\n\\n[list all modified/created files]\\n\\n## Deferred Items\\n\\n[list or \\\"None\\\"]\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Orchestration Notes\\n\\n### Error Handling\\n\\nIf any phase encounters issues:\\n- Stop execution at that phase\\n- Report the issue clearly\\n- Preserve partial progress via git commits\\n- Wait for Product Owner clarification\\n- Can resume by re-invoking this manager\\n\\n### State Management\\n\\n- PROGRESS_BOARD.md tracks Sprint and Backlog Item states\\n- Each phase updates its respective sections\\n- Git commits serve as synchronization checkpoints\\n\\n### Phase Independence\\n\\nIndividual agents can still be invoked separately:\\n- Useful for resuming after manual fixes\\n- Allows targeted phase re-execution\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Execution Checklist\\n\\n**IMPORTANT**: The RUP Manager MUST complete ALL 6 steps:\\n\\n- [ ] **Step 0** — Mode + test params detected; banner displayed\\n- [ ] **Phase 1** — Setup (contract + analysis) → `sprint_N_setup.md` → commit, push\\n- [ ] **Phase 2** — Design + test spec + skeletons → `sprint_N_design.md` + `new_tests.manifest` → commit, push\\n- [ ] **Phase 3** — Construction (no new tests) → `sprint_N_implementation.md` → commit, push\\n- [ ] **Phase 4** — Quality gates (Phase A + Phase B) → log files + `sprint_N_tests.md` → commit, push\\n- [ ] **Phase 5** — Wrap-up (README + backlog traceability) → commit, push\\n- [ ] **Step 6** — Final Summary (MANDATORY — never skip)\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Related Rules\\n\\n| Rule File | Purpose |\\n|-----------|---------|\\n| Designer's testing strategy template |\\n| `rules/generic/test_procedures.md` | Test Architect + Executor procedures |\\n| `rules/generic/test_failure_classification.md` | Flaky vs broken handling |\\n| `rules/generic/bug_policy.md` | Bug fold-in vs promote |\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Usage\\n\\nTo execute a complete RUP cycle:\\n\\n1. Ensure PLAN.md has a Sprint with `Status: Progress` and required fields (`Mode:`, `Test:`, `Regression:`)\\n2. Invoke this manager: `/rup-manager`\\n3. Manager automatically executes all 6 steps\\n4. Verify all checklist items are completed\\n\\n**Note**: For manual phase control, invoke individual agent commands directly (see `agents/README.md`).\\n# Contractor Agent - Contracting Phase Specialist\\n\\n**Role**: Establish Agent Cooperation Specification and confirm understanding of project rules.\\n\\n**Phase**: 1/5 - Contracting\\n\\n## Do not repeat yourself  \\n\\nIf in current session contracting was already completed - do not execute this phase, as contracting was already executed. Make a note that contracting was already done in previous Sprint. You my add symbolic link to the actual contracting confirmation.\\n\\n## Responsibilities\\n\\n1. Review project scope in BACKLOG.md\\n2. Review implementation plan in PLAN.md\\n3. Review all rules in `rules/generic` directory\\n4. Apply rules in `rules/specific` only to technologies explicitly selected for the project\\n5. Confirm understanding of cooperation rules\\n6. Document any unclear points or conflicts\\n7. Summarize understanding and readiness\\n\\n## Execution\\n\\nExecute the contracting phase by following these instructions:\\n\\n### Step 1: Read Foundation Documents\\n\\nRead the following documents to understand the project:\\n\\n1. `AGENTS.md` - General instructions for agents\\n2. `BACKLOG.md` - Project scope and requirements\\n3. `PLAN.md` - Implementation plan and Sprint organization\\n4. `progress/` directory - Previously completed work\\n\\n### Step 2: Review Cooperation Rules\\n\\nRead and confirm understanding of all rules:\\n\\n**Generic Rules (technology-agnostic):**\\n1. `rules/generic/GENERAL_RULES.md` - General cooperation rules\\n2. `rules/generic/PRODUCT_OWNER_GUIDE.md` - Product Owner workflow\\n3. `rules/generic/GIT_RULES.md` - Git repository rules\\n\\n**Technology-Specific Rules (based on project):**\\n4. `rules/<technology>/` directory - Check for technology-specific development rules\\n   - For GitHub Actions projects: `rules/github_actions/GitHub_DEV_RULES.md`\\n   - For Ansible projects: `rules/ansible/` (future)\\n   - For Terraform projects: `rules/terraform/` (future)\\n\\n### Step 3: Validate Understanding\\n\\nFor each document reviewed:\\n- Summarize key points\\n- Identify any unclear or conflicting requirements\\n- Note any concerns or potential issues\\n- Confirm compliance capability\\n\\n### Step 4: Enumerate Responsibilities\\n\\nList your responsibilities as an Implementor:\\n- What you are allowed to edit\\n- What you must never modify\\n- How to propose changes\\n- How to ask questions\\n- Status tokens and state machines\\n- Git commit requirements\\n\\n### Step 5: Document Understanding\\n\\nCreate a contracting summary that includes:\\n\\n1. **Project Overview**: Brief description of the project goals\\n2. **Current Sprint**: Identify Sprint(s) in `Progress` status\\n3. **Key Requirements**: Summary of active Backlog Items\\n4. **Rule Compliance**: Confirmation of understanding for each rule document\\n5. **Responsibilities**: Clear enumeration of Implementor role\\n6. **Constraints**: List of prohibited actions\\n7. **Communication Protocol**: How to propose changes and ask questions\\n8. **Open Questions**: Any unclear points requiring clarification\\n\\n### Step 6: Create Contracting Summary\\n\\nOnce the contract is clear:\\n\\n- collect statistics about LLM tokens used for this phase to be added the phase summary\\n- save your contracting summary `progress/sprint_${no}/sprint_${no}_contract.md`\\n\\n### Step 6: Finalize\\n\\n**If anything is unclear:**\\n- Stop here\\n- List all open questions\\n- Wait for clarifications from Product Owner\\n- Do NOT proceed to commit\\n\\n**If everything is clear:**\\n- Confirm your readiness to proceed\\n- State: \\\"Contracting phase complete - ready for Inception\\\"\\n- Commit the contracting review file following semantic commit conventions\\n- Use commit message format: `docs: sprint_${no} contracting phase completed`\\n\\n## Completion Criteria\\n\\nThe Contractor Agent has successfully completed when:\\n\\n- [x] All foundation documents read and understood\\n- [x] All rule documents read and understood\\n- [x] Responsibilities enumerated clearly\\n- [x] Constraints and prohibited actions identified\\n- [x] Communication protocols understood\\n- [x] Open questions documented (if any)\\n- [x] Contracting summary saved in proper location\\n- [x] Changes committed with semantic commit message\\n- [x] Readiness confirmed or clarifications requested\\n- [x] LLM tokens statistics collected and save to file\\n\\n## Output Format\\n\\nYour final output should be:\\n\\n```markdown\\n# Contracting Phase - Status Report\\n\\n## Summary\\n[Brief overview of what was reviewed]\\n\\n## Understanding Confirmed\\n- Project scope: [Yes/No - with summary]\\n- Implementation plan: [Yes/No - with summary]\\n- General rules: [Yes/No - with summary]\\n- Git rules: [Yes/No - with summary]\\n- Development rules: [Yes/No - with summary]\\n\\n## Responsibilities Enumerated\\n[List key responsibilities]\\n\\n## Open Questions\\n[List any unclear points, or state \\\"None\\\"]\\n\\n## Status\\n[Contracting Complete - Ready for Inception | Awaiting Clarification]\\n\\n## Artifacts Created\\n- progress/sprint_${no}/sprint_${no}_contract_review_${cnt}.md\\n\\n## Next Phase\\n[Inception Phase | Awaiting Clarification]\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n**Note**: This agent is specialized for the Contracting phase only.       "aggregated_output": "# E.18 setup\n\n## Contract\n\nThe Product Owner explicitly authorizes activation, YOLO execution, companion repositories and final origin push. Only E.18 and necessary shared documents change. SQLcl PROJECT and Actions retain native responsibilities. No credentials are stored in scratch. No prior completion assertion counts as fresh evidence. Token accounting is unavailable.\n\n## Analysis\n\n[PBI-086](../../BACKLOG.md#pbi-086-measure-complete-pipeline-feedback-and-runner-capacity) and [PLAN](../../PLAN.md#sprint-e18---measure-complete-pipeline-feedback-and-runner-capacity) require complete workload timing on the E.17 baseline. E.17 source has JDK, pinned SQLcl, Oracle, APEX, ORDS and native release checks; utPLSQL must be added to the measured workload. Fresh hosted jobs preserve candidate isolation. The primary actor is SRS A-03 Delivery operator, supported by A-05 Git hosting service; browser validation exercises A-04 application-user behavior. Actions, Oracle and the project-owned pipeline remain internal components under the [SRS actor model](../../docs/srs.md#actors). The operator dispatches a declared experiment, inspects phase/resource/queue receipts, compares distributions and chooses capacity without dropping validation.\n\n## Decisions\n\nUse an Elaboration executable measurement prototype, retaining all E.17 native checks. Use two replicas per serial/concurrent trial and explicitly describe small-sample percentile limits. Cold means a unique empty per-run dependency-cache key; warm means a verified cache hit, not a reused database. Database state remains job-private. No ten-minute process cutoff supersedes the user instruction to persist.\n# E.18 implementation record\n\n## Current verified state\n\nMeasured source capacity-v1 at aea3a77 passes all four two-replica cells. [Report](capacity_report.md) derives timings/resources/recommendations from actual receipts; [checklist](sprint_E.18_checklist.md) owns acceptance coverage. Separate live revision 920b1c4 passes both complete replicas and preserves blank-login/final-result captures. All six new/full gates pass, including unfiltered 27/27 integration with stable executable-source snapshots and scoped cleanup. Presentation/handover technical review passes; the final audit and lifecycle transaction are recorded in the handover and closure records. The historical trial sections below are retained observations, not the current status.\n\n## Initial exploratory execution — historical\n\nDedicated repository: https://github.com/rstyczynski/APEX_CICD_E.18 at f2be037f2bbd6a3f8757954077e6c9c811f5d041. Maintained source is mirrored in [sprint-work/E.18](../../sprint-work/E.18/). [Design](sprint_E.18_design.md) owns measurement definitions and [checklist](sprint_E.18_checklist.md) tracks acceptance.\n\nCold serial run 45844508366, cold concurrent run 45844673934 and warm-cache population run 45844682312 are dispatched. All are pending. Population runs are excluded from warm measurements. Jobs run on independent hosted machines; concurrency here measures runner slots and batch completion, not competing databases on one host. Aggregate host counters include the runner and background services. Five-second sampling can miss short peaks. GitHub job start minus workflow creation combines scheduling, capacity queueing and deliberate serial admission; it does not isolate GitHub infrastructure queueing.\n\nThe observer records CPU counters, available memory and root-disk free space without collecting process command lines or credentials. Actions API step timestamps provide phase durations; native result files validate successful work. Download caches are verified by the original archive checksums on every use. Database images and utPLSQL source remain pinned. JDK retains E.17's major-version setup policy; exact installed build is a reported measurement input, not an invented immutable pin.\n\n## Initial source checks\n\nA1 source smoke and A2 arithmetic contracts pass 1/1 each. They establish syntax, named E.17 step retention and report arithmetic only. Native execution and cache-hit qualification remain pending. No production performance conclusion follows from these checks.\n\n## UI capture recovery\n\nThe browser connector returned “The browser access host is unavailable”. Native Chrome control was available and used to inspect the real first trial. [Gallery](screenshots/index.html) preserves the starting window and framed copy. The failed connector did not block UI inspection.\n\n## Corrected trials\n\nAfter preserving the initial installer failure, source d878df0 dispatches corrected cold serial 45845297753, cold concurrent 45845306173 and cache population 45845314659. Initial exploratory trials preceded the persisted design specification and are not acceptance samples. The corrected trials follow that specification. The native installer log redacts its vendor-default schema password; job-generated private passwords remain masked.\n\n## Representative backend workload and invoker authority\n\nSource 1470fb3 replaces the exploratory arithmetic-only test with the existing five-case backend/API fixtures, unchanged from tests/sql/backend and examples/testable-backend. Source aea3a77 executes the suite as E5_TEST, avoiding SYS invoker inheritance. Current cold serial 45846143379, cold concurrent 45846150456 and cache population 45846157988 follow the persisted design; each starts independent databases and retains the full E.17 lifecycle. Earlier trial IDs are diagnostic only.\n\n## Measured-source freeze and live capture revision\n\nBoth cache population replicas in 45846157988 pass. The measured source is frozen as tag capacity-v1 at aea3a771cac687fa32ac09d24faf6d2d55bd2342. Warm serial 45848134832 and warm concurrent 45848140354 dispatch that exact tag, matching cold trials 45846143379/45846150456. Warm population is excluded. Live demonstration 45848145737 dispatches separate main source 920b1c4, which adds a blank-login screenshot before entering private inputs. Its timings are excluded from the comparison. This preserves source equivalence for all eight measured samples while recording the complete application UI transition in the demonstration.\naccess-verification.log\napex_24.2.zip\napex-admin-verified.png\napex-configure.log\napex-http-body.html\napex-http-headers.txt\napex-install.log\napex-login.html\napex-version.txt\nauthor-state.json\nauthor-url.txt\nbrowser-endpoint.txt\ncaptures\nclean-clone-review\ncontainers.conf\ncredentials.env\ndb-start.log\ndb-verification.log\ndeveloper-results-rehearsal.log\ndeveloper-walkthrough-rehearsal.log\ndevelopers\ne1-final-preflight.log\ne1-owned.json\ne1-secrets.json\ne15\ne16\ne17\ne17-candidates\ne17-repo\ne18-deck\ne18-repo\ne2\ne3\ne4\ne5\ne6\nenvironment-rename-20261018\nfoundation-http-cookies.txt\nfoundation-runtime-check.html\nfull-clean-clone\nfull-clean-clone-inputs.log\nhandover-build\nhandover-final\nhelper\nidentity-review.bux6va3j\nidentity-review.vkmx0otg\nkrunkit-1.3.2.tgz\nkrunkit-entitlements.plist\nkrunkit-usage.md\noperator\noperator-rehearsal.log\nords-doc.html\nords-install-help.txt\nords-install.log\nords-landing.html\nords-repair.log\nqualify-corrected-patch.log\nqualify-corrected-patch.sh\nrecovery\nrecovery-proof.log\nrehearsal-vm-start.log\nrehearsal-vm-start.plist\nreleases\nrestart-verification.log\nretained-before-rehearsal\nrun-sql\nstatic-response.txt\ntest-cookies.txt\ntools\nverify-cookies.txt\nvm-start.log\nvm-start.plist\nwalkthrough\nwip\nwrite-clean-proof.py\nwrong-operator-target.log\n/Users/rstyczynski/.codex/sessions/2026/11/26/rollout-2026-11-26T15-59-25-01a2185e-54e7-7761-9f44-f2a3c8486ca8.jsonl\n/Users/rstyczynski/.codex/sessions/2026/11/26/rollout-2026-11-26T09-58-42-01a21714-1536-77f2-b970-c1a9f535f886.jsonl\n/Users/rstyczynski/.codex/sessions/2026/11/26/rollout-2026-11-26T10-26-11-01a2172d-3f62-7c03-9cfb-fa381a088d96.jsonl\n/Users/rstyczynski/.codex/sessions/2026/11/26/rollout-2026-11-26T08-55-39-01a216da-5f4a-7302-912f-89a0d663ff0f.jsonl\n/Users/rstyczynski/.codex/sessions/2026/11/26/rollout-2026-11-26T12-23-53-01a21799-035f-72c3-ad2d-93a52ed5e89a.jsonl\n/Users/rstyczynski/.codex/sessions/2026/11/26/ All are pending. Population runs are excluded from warm measurements. Jobs run on independent hosted machines; concurrency here measures runner slots and batch completion, not competing databases on one host. Aggregate host counters include the runner and background services. Five-second sampling can miss short peaks. GitHub job start minus workflow creation combines scheduling, capacity queueing and deliberate serial admission; it does not isolate GitHub infrastructure queueing.\\n\\nThe observer records CPU counters, available memory and root-disk free space without collecting process command lines or credentials. Actions API step timestamps provide phase durations; native result files validate successful work. Download caches are verified by the original archive checksums on every use. Database images and utPLSQL source remain pinned. JDK retains E.17's major-version setup policy; exact installed build is a reported measurement input, not an invented immutable pin.\\n\\n## Initial source checks\\n\\nA1 source smoke and A2 arithmetic contracts pass 1/1 each. They establish syntax, named E.17 step retention and report arithmetic only. Native execution and cache-hit qualification remain pending. No production performance conclusion follows from these checks.\\n\\n## UI capture recovery\\n\\nThe browser connector returned “The browser access host is unavailable”. Native Chrome control was available and used to inspect the real first trial. [Gallery](screenshots/index.html) preserves the starting window and framed copy. The failed connector did not block UI inspection.\\n\\n## Corrected trials\\n\\nAfter preserving the initial installer failure, source d878df0 dispatches corrected cold serial 45845297753, cold concurrent 45845306173 and cache population 45845314659. Initial exploratory trials preceded the persisted design specification and are not acceptance samples. The corrected trials follow that specification. The native installer log redacts its vendor-default schema password; job-generated private passwords remain masked.\\n\\n## Representative backend workload and invoker authority\\n\\nSource 1470fb3 replaces the exploratory arithmetic-only test with the existing five-case backend/API fixtures, unchanged from tests/sql/backend and examples/testable-backend. Source aea3a77 executes the suite as E5_TEST, avoiding SYS invoker inheritance. Current cold serial 45846143379, cold concurrent 45846150456 and cache population 45846157988 follow the persisted design; each starts independent databases and retains the full E.17 lifecycle. Earlier trial IDs are diagnostic only.\\n\\n## Measured-source freeze and live capture revision\\n\\nBoth cache population replicas in 45846157988 pass. The measured source is frozen as tag capacity-v1 at aea3a771cac687fa32ac09d24faf6d2d55bd2342. Warm serial 45848134832 and warm concurrent 45848140354 dispatch that exact tag, matching cold trials 45846143379/45846150456. Warm population is excluded. Live demonstration 45848145737 dispatches separate main source 920b1c4, which adds a blank-login screenshot before entering private inputs. Its timings are excluded from the comparison. This preserves source equivalence for all eight measured samples while recording the complete application UI transition in the This is a local policy;\nit does not change the canonical method submodule.\n\n## P2. Project lifecycle phases set emphasis; they do not prohibit refinement\n\nThe project lifecycle remains Inception, Elaboration, Construction, and Transition. These phases establish the dominant emphasis of the work:\n\n- Inception emphasizes business context, vision, requirements, scope, and major risks.\n- Elaboration emphasizes architecture, risk retirement, design, and validation.\n- Construction emphasizes implementation and testing.\n- Transition emphasizes deployment, stabilization, and user feedback.\n\nAll disciplines remain open to refinement in later iterations. In particular, business modeling and requirements are mostly established early, but evidence from design, implementation, testing, or use may require them to be revisited.\n\n## P3. Sprint wrap-up is not necessarily project Transition\n\nThe RUP Strikes Back wrap-up phase completes and documents a sprint. It does not by itself mean that the product has entered the project-level Transition phase or is ready for release. Project-level Transition occurs when the project plan declares release, deployment, and adoption activities to be the dominant focus.\n\n## P4. A sprint may deliver multiple coherent outcomes\n\nA sprint may contain multiple independently coherent backlog items when they share a common iteration objective, have manageable dependencies, and can be reviewed together. This is particularly appropriate for Inception and Elaboration, where one iteration can establish several connected artifacts such as requirements, risks, scope, and an architecture baseline.\n\nEach backlog item must retain its own value statement and acceptance signal. The sprint design and wrap-up must identify the evidence for each item, so grouping does not hide incomplete work or weaken traceability. Split items into separate sprints when their dependencies, risk, approval needs, or validation methods would make a single review unclear.\n\n## P5. Process entry point and local method adoption\n\nThe project root `BACKLOG.md` is the source of Product Owner priorities. The project root `PLAN.md` is the source of sprint selection and status. When installed, the root `RUP_patch.md` points to `RUPStrikesBack.patch/RUP_patch.md`, which defines local policy that supplements or overrides the generic method in the `RUPStrikesBack` submodule.\n\nSprint 0 is marked `Progress` by the bootstrap plan. To execute it, the Product Owner explicitly invokes the Codex `rup-strikes-back` skill in the declared mode. For a later planned sprint, the Product Owner first changes that sprint’s status in the root `PLAN.md` from `Planned` to `Progress`, then explicitly invokes the skill. The agent reads its manager and selected phase procedures from `.agents/skills/rup-strikes-back/references/`, then applies this local patch before executing the sprint.\n\n`rup-strikes-back` is the Codex method entry point. The submodule's `/rup-manager` is a Claude compatibility command, not a native Codex command. Managed mode remains the default: the agent pauses for Product Owner approval at the defined decision points.\n\n## P6. Refine prototype work into hierarchical sub-PBIs\n\nWhen an accepted Inception architecture identifies a prototype PBI that spans\nseveral independently reviewable pieces of work, the following Elaboration\niteration must refine that parent PBI before prototype construction. This is a\nrequired planning checkpoint, not permission to invent the work during\nInception.\n\nUse hierarchical identifiers in the form `PBI-XX.YY`, where `PBI-XX` is the\naccepted parent PBI and `YY` is a sequential child number. For example,\n`PBI-011.1`, `PBI-011.2`, and `PBI-011.3` are child work items of `PBI-011`.\n\nCreate the child items during the Elaboration sprint's setup/design phase, in\nthe active sprint's design record. Each child item must state its parent, its\nbounded outcome, its acceptance evidence, and its dependency on other child\nitems. Record the same identifiers on the progress board for status tracking.\n\nChild sub-PBIs are sprint-scoped refinements of their parent, not new\nProduct-Owner backlog items. Keep the parent PBI in the root `BACKLOG.md` and\n`PLAN.md`; promote a child to a new root backlog item only when it gains\nindependent product value, priority, or deferral authority. In managed mode,\nthe Product Owner approves the proposed decomposition before prototype\nconstruction starts.\ncat: RUPStrikesBack/rules/github_actions/GitHub_DEV_RULES.md: No such file or directory\n# Git repository rules\n\n1. Use semantic commit messages: https://gist.github.com/joshbuchea/6f47e86d2510bce28f8e7f42ae84c716\n\n2. Semantic massage does not include any thing before `:`, it's place fro just type of change.\n\n    It's forbidden:\n\n    ```text\n    docs(sprint-14): add functional tests documentation and snippet status table\n    ```\n\n    It's correct:\n\n    ```text\n    docs: (sprint-14) add functional tests documentation and snippet status table\n    ```\n\n3. Push to remote after commit.\n",

      "aggregated_output": "#!/usr/bin/env bash\n# One SQLcl invocation; Actions owns sequencing and all lifecycle decisions.\nset -euo pipefail\nconnection=$1\nlabel=$2\nmkdir -p \"$GITHUB_WORKSPACE/result\"\n\"$SQL_EXE\" -version > \"$GITHUB_WORKSPACE/result/tool.txt\" 2>&1\npython3 \"$GITHUB_WORKSPACE/check_tool.py\" \"$GITHUB_WORKSPACE/result/tool.txt\"\ninput=$(mktemp \"$RUNNER_TEMP/native-input.XXXXXX\")\ntrap 'rm -f \"$input\"' EXIT\n{\n  printf '%s\\n' 'whenever sqlerror exit failure rollback' 'whenever oserror exit failure rollback'\n  if [[ \"$connection\" != - ]]; then printf 'connect -name %s\\n' \"$connection\"; fi\n  cat\n  printf '\\nexit\\n'\n} > \"$input\"\ntimeout 240 \"$SQL_EXE\" -home \"$RUNNER_TEMP/private-sqlcl\" -nohistory -noupdates -S -L /nolog < \"$input\" 2>&1 | tee \"$GITHUB_WORKSPACE/result/$label.log\"\npython3 \"$GITHUB_WORKSPACE/check_output.py\" \"$GITHUB_WORKSPACE/result/$label.log\"\n\"\"\"Trusted candidate validator; candidate input is SQL data, never host code.\"\"\"\nimport os\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\ndef load(path):\n    p = Path(path)\n    if p.name != 'candidate.sql' or p.is_symlink():\n        raise ValueError('only candidate.sql regular input accepted')\n    source = p.read_text()\n    if len(source) > 8192 or not source.strip():\n        raise ValueError('empty or oversized SQL')\n    return source\n\ndef main():\n    import oracledb\n    source = load('candidate.sql')\n    with oracledb.connect(user='candidate', password=os.environ['CANDIDATE_PASSWORD'],\n                         dsn='localhost:1521/FREEPDB1') as conn:\n        conn.call_timeout = 30000\n        with conn.cursor() as cur:\n            cur.execute(source)\n            rows = cur.fetchall()\n            assert rows == [('Hello World',)], rows\n            cur.execute(\"select sys_context('USERENV','SESSION_USER') from dual\")\n            assert cur.fetchone() == ('CANDIDATE',)\n            try:\n                cur.execute('select * from system.e15_private')\n            except oracledb.DatabaseError as exc:\n                assert exc.args[0].code == 942, exc\n            else:\n                raise AssertionError('private table readable')\n            print('PASS real Oracle greeting, schema identity and private-table refusal')\n\nif __name__ == '__main__':\n    main()\nsprint-finalize.sh\n",
      "exit_code": 0,

        "secs": 0,
        "nanos": 56342375
      }

Export: {ts}. Initial execution; no --continue observed.','', 'Coverage: all complete JSONL records accessible at snapshot time. Native writer may lag; records after export are absent. No completeness claim. Credential-shaped values explicitly redacted where matched.','']\\nfor i,r in enumerate(parsed,1):\\n md.extend([f'## Record {i}: {r.get(\\\"type\\\",\\\"unknown\\\")}','', '```json',json.dumps(r,ensure_ascii=False,indent=2),'```',''])\\n(root/(base+'.md')).write_text('\\\\n'.join(md))\\nindex=root/'index.md'\\nif not index.exists():index.write_text('# E.19 chat snapshots\\\\n\\\\nNative local session JSONL read interface. Snapshots preserve accessible records chronologically; readable companions retain structured content. No reconstruction. Coverage excludes pending writer buffers, future messages and any inaccessible records.\\\\n\\\\n')\\nwith index.open('a') as f:f.write(f'- [{base}.jsonl]({base}.jsonl), [readable]({base}.md): thread {thread}; exported {ts}; initial execution; records 1–{len(records)} across {len(files)} matching native files; writer lag and later messages excluded.\\\\n')\\nprint(f'Archived {len(records)} records from {len(files)} matching files')\\nPY\\npython3 progress/E.19/archive_chat.py\\ncat > progress/E.19/sprint_E.19_design.md <<'EOF'\\n# E.19 design\\n\\n## Scope and responsibilities\\n\\nImplement reusable Node actions for tool compatibility, native output and provenance, then qualify all remaining workflow Python support by inventory. Do not claim the initial three-action slice exhausts PBI-087. Use explicit exceptions, required input validation, regular-file checks and SHA-256. Actions return a validated output only on success and fail before any deployment action can run. Each action performs one check; workflows retain sequencing. No OCI interaction is selected in this slice.\\n\\n## Child outcomes\\n\\nPBI-087.1 (parent PBI-087): inventory and catalogue every current workflow Python dependency, with reasoned dispositions; acceptance is complete inventory and callable catalogue. PBI-087.2 depends on .1: migrate checks with input/refusal contracts and hosted action execution. PBI-087.3 depends on .2: equivalent runtime comparison, full regression, demonstration and audited handover. All remain unfinished until their evidence exists.\\n\\n### Testing Strategy\\n\\nSmoke loads each action and checks metadata. Unit tests exercise valid and missing/malformed inputs, exact tool/metadata version and qualified build, ANSI-stripped native error signatures, source/artifact/build mismatches and explicit failure independent of optimization. Integration invokes catalogued actions in real GitHub jobs with deployment downstream of valid gates; rejected checks must skip target mutation. Synthetic fixtures establish check contracts, not Oracle delivery. Compare Python baseline and JavaScript on identical bytes, runner image and repetitions; report setup, check and workflow separately. Full unfiltered smoke/unit/integration regression remains required.\\n\\n## Test Specification\\n\\nSM-19: metadata and Node source load. UT-19: positive and negative contracts for each migrated check. IT-19: actual hosted uses of each catalogue action, downstream mutation sentinel refusal, real tool identity qualification. PERF-19: cold/warm comparable samples and explicit limitations. UI-19: captures during actual inspection, local gallery navigation/link validation and rendered slides. ARCHIVE-19: timestamped native session exports with exact accessible coverage. No unrun case is a pass.\\n\\n## Decision\\n\\nYOLO design self-approval applies to this initial slice. Broader inventory can refine migration before implementation of additional actions. Neither fixture tests nor three pure checks alone establish parent acceptance.\\nEOF\",\"max_output_tokens\":1000}));\n",
